We are now ready to introduce the auxiliary leave-one-out procedure for subspace estimation. Similar to the matrix
\bm{B}
in Algorithm
2
(whose eigenspace serves as an estimate of the column space of
\bm{U}^{\star}
), we define an auxiliary matrix
\bm{B}^{\left(m\right)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{B}^{\left(m\right)}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathsf{off}\text{-}\mathsf{diag}}\big(\bm{A}^{\left(m\right)}\bm{A}^{\left(m\right)\top}\big),

(62)

where

\mathcal{P}_{\mathsf{off}\text{-}\mathsf{diag}}(\cdot)
(as already defined in Section
3.1
) extracts out off-diagonal entries from a matrix.
The rationale is simple: it can be easily verified that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{B}^{\left(m\right)}\big]=\bm{B}^{\star}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathsf{diag}}\left(\bm{B}^{\star}\right),\qquad\bm{B}^{\star}:=\bm{A}^{\star}\bm{A}^{\star\top},

(63)

where
\mathcal{P}_{\mathsf{diag}}(\cdot)
extracts out the diagonal entries of the matrix.
This gives hope that the eigenspace of
\bm{B}^{\left(m\right)}
is also a reliable estimate of the column space of
\bm{U}^{\star}
, provided that the diagonal entries of
\bm{B}^{\star}
are sufficiently small.
Consequently, we shall compute
\bm{U}^{0,\left(m\right)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
— a matrix whose columns are the top-
r
leading eigenvectors of
\bm{B}^{\left(m\right)}
. The procedure is summarized in Algorithm
5
.

Algorithm 5

The
m
-th leave-one-out sequence for spectral initialization

1:

Let
\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)}\bm{\Lambda}^{\left(m\right)}\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)\top}
be the rank-
r
eigen-decomposition of
\bm{B}^{\left(m\right)}
defined in (
62
).

2:

Generate the initial estimate
\bm{U}^{0,\left(m\right)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
from
\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
using Algorithm
6
.

The following lemma plays a crucial role in our analysis, which formalizes the fact that the leave-one-out version
\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)}
obtained by Algorithm
5
is extremely close to
\bm{U}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

There exist some universal constants
c_{0},c_{1},c_{2}>0

such that if

\displaystyle p\geq c_{0}\frac{\mu^{2}r\log^{2}d}{d^{3/2}},\quad\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\leq c_{1}\frac{\sqrt{p}}{d^{3/4}\sqrt{\log d}},\quad\text{and}\quad r\leq c_{2}\sqrt{\frac{d}{\mu}},

then with probability
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, the subspace estimate
\bm{U}^{(m)}
computed by Algorithm
5
obeys

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}-\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)}\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)\top}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}}

(64)

simultaneously for all
1\leq m\leq d
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}:=\frac{\mu^{2}r\log d}{d^{3/2}p}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r\log d}{d^{2}p}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2}}\frac{d^{3/2}\log d}{p}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}.

(65)

Lemma
5.8
follows immediately from the analysis of
[
CLC+20
, Lemma 4]
. As a remark, the construction of the leave-one-out sequences herein is slightly different from the one in
[
CLC+20
]
.
However, it is straightforward to adapt the proof of
[
CLC+20
]
to the case considered herein. We therefore omit the proof for the sake of brevity.

5.3
Analysis for initialization: Part 2 (retrieval of individual tensor factors)

5.3.1
Main results and leave-one-out sequences

This section justifies that the procedure presented in Algorithm
3
allows to disentangle the tensor factors.
For notational simplicity, we let

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}:=\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\,/\left\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2},\qquad\lambda_{i}^{\star}:=\left\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{3},\qquad 1\leq i\leq d.

(66)

Our result is this:

Theorem 5.9
.

Fix any arbitrary small constant
\delta>0
. Assume that

\displaystyle p\geq c_{0}\frac{\mu^{2}r^{4}\log^{4}d}{d^{3/2}},\quad\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\leq c_{1}\frac{\sqrt{p}}{r^{3/2}d^{3/4}\log^{2}d},\quad r\leq c_{2}\left(\frac{d}{\mu^{6}\log^{6}d}\right)^{1/6},

\displaystyle L=c_{3}r^{2\kappa^{2}}\log^{3/2}r,\quad\epsilon_{\mathsf{th}}=c_{4}\Bigg\{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\Bigg\}

(67)

for some sufficiently large universal constant
c_{0},c_{3}>0
and some sufficiently small universal constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{4}>0
.
Then with probability exceeding
1-\delta
, there exists a permutation
\pi(\cdot):[d]\mapsto[d]
such that for all
1\leq i\leq r
, the tensor factors
\{\bm{w}^{i}\}_{i=1}^{r}
returned by Algorithm
3
satisfy

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{w}^{i}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{\pi(i)}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu r\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}};

(68a)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{w}^{i}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{\pi(i)}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu^{2}r\log^{4}d}{d^{3/2}p}+\frac{\mu r\log^{3}d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2}}\frac{d^{3/2}\log^{4}d}{p}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{6}d}{p}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log^{2}d}{d}}\Bigg\}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}};

(68b)

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{\pi(i)}^{\star}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\Bigg\}\lambda^{\star}_{\pi(i)}.

(68c)

In short, this theorem asserts that the estimates returned by Algorithm
3
are — up to global permutation — reasonably close to the ground truth under our sample size and noise conditions. In order to establish this theorem and in order to provide initial guesses for the leave-one-out GD sequences, we need to produce a leave-one-out sequence tailored to this part of the algorithm. Such auxiliary sequences are generated in a similar spirit as the previous ones, and we summarize them in Algorithm
6
. As usual, the resulting leave-one-out estimates
\big\{\lambda^{\left(m\right)}_{i},\bm{w}^{i,\left(m\right)}\big\}_{i=1}^{r}
are statistically independent of
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{m}}\left(\bm{E}\right)
.

In what follows, we gather a few key properties of the leave-one-out estimates, which play a crucial role in the analysis.

Algorithm 6

The
m
-th leave-one-out sequence for retrieving individual tensor components

1:

Input:
restart number
L
, threshold
\epsilon_{\mathsf{th}}
, subspace estimate
\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
given by Algorithm
5
.

2:

for

\tau=1,\dots,L

do

3:

Recall the Gaussian vector
\bm{g}^{\tau}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\bm{I}_{d})
generated in Algorithm
3
.

4:

\big(\bm{\nu}^{\tau,\left(m\right)},\lambda_{\tau}^{\left(m\right)},\mathsf{spec}\text{-}\mathsf{gap}_{\tau}^{\left(m\right)}\big)\leftarrow\textsc{Retrieve-one-tensor-factor}(\bm{T}^{\left(m\right)},p,\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)},\bm{g}^{\tau})
.

5:

Generate tensor factor estimates

\displaystyle\big\{\big(\bm{w}^{1,\left(m\right)},\lambda_{1}^{\left(m\right)}),\dots,(\bm{w}^{r,\left(m\right)},\lambda_{r}^{\left(m\right)}\big)\big\}\leftarrow\textsc{Prune}(\big\{\big(\bm{\nu}^{\tau,\left(m\right)},\lambda_{\tau}^{\left(m\right)},\mathsf{spec}\text{-}\mathsf{gap}_{\tau}^{\left(m\right)}\big)\big\}_{\tau=1}^{L},\epsilon_{\mathsf{th}}).

6:

Output:
an initial estimate
\bm{U}^{0,\left(m\right)}=\big[\big(\lambda_{1}^{\left(m\right)}\big)^{1/3}\bm{w}^{1,\left(m\right)},\dots,\big(\lambda_{r}^{\left(m\right)}\big)^{1/3}\bm{w}^{r,\left(m\right)}\big].

Theorem 5.10
.

Fix any arbitrarily small constant
\delta>0
. Instate the assumptions in Theorem
5.9
.
With probability exceeding
1-\delta
, the permutation function stated in Theorem
5.9
obeys that: for all
1\leq i\leq r
and all
1\leq m\leq d
:

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{w}^{i}-\bm{w}^{i,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu^{2}r\log^{3/2}d}{d^{3/2}p}+\frac{\mu\sqrt{r}\,\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2}}\frac{d^{3/2}\log^{3/2}d}{p}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log^{2}d}{p}}\Bigg\}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}};

(69a)

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\left(m\right)}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu^{2}r\log^{3/2}d}{d^{3/2}p}+\frac{\mu\sqrt{r}\,\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2}}\frac{d^{3/2}\log^{3/2}d}{p}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log^{2}d}{p}}\Bigg\}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star};

(69b)

\displaystyle\big|\big(\bm{w}^{i,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{\pi(i)}\big)_{m}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\sqrt{\mu r}\log^{7/2}d}{d^{3/2}p}+\frac{\mu r\log^{3}d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\frac{\log^{4}d}{p}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{6}d}{p}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log^{2}d}{d}}\Bigg\}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}}.

(69c)

With Theorems
5.9
-
5.10
in place, we can immediately establish a few desired properties (particularly those specified in Section
5.1
) of our initial estimate, as asserted in the following corollary.

Corollary 5.11
.

Fix any arbitrarily small constant
\delta>0
. Instate the assumptions in Theorem
2.8
. With probability exceeding
1-\delta
, the estimates
\bm{U}^{0}
and
\bm{U}^{0,\left(m\right)}
returned by Algorithm
3
and Algorithm
6
respectively satisfy the hypotheses (
47
) for
t=0
.

Proof.

See Appendix
B.10
.
∎

5.3.2
Analysis

Before we start with the proof, we first state the main idea. For the sake of clarify, we define

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{\tau}

\displaystyle:=\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{g}^{\tau},

(70a)

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{\tau,(m)}

\displaystyle:=\bm{U}^{(m)}\bm{U}^{(m)\top}\bm{g}^{\tau},

(70b)

\displaystyle\bm{M}^{\tau}

\displaystyle:=p^{-1}\bm{T}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau},

(70c)

\displaystyle\bm{M}^{\tau,(m)}

\displaystyle:=p^{-1}\bm{T}^{(m)}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau,(m)}.

(70d)

In addition, let
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
be the top singular vector of
\bm{M}^{\tau}
obeying
\langle\bm{T},(\bm{\nu}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\rangle\geq 0
, and
\bm{\nu}^{\tau,(m)}
the top singular vector of
\bm{M}^{\tau,(m)}
obeying
\langle\bm{T}^{(m)},(\bm{\nu}^{\tau,(m)})^{\otimes 3}\rangle\geq 0
. Set

\displaystyle\lambda_{\tau}:=\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\bm{\nu}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\rangle\qquad\text{and}\qquad\lambda_{\tau}^{(m)}:=\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}^{(m)},(\bm{\nu}^{\tau,(m)})^{\otimes 3}\rangle.

(71)

These are all computed in the function
\textsc{Retrieve-one-tensor-factor}(
) in the
\tau
-th round.

1.

We first show that for each
1\leq i\leq r
, there exists at least one trial
1\leq\tau\leq L
such that the
i
-th tensor factor
\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}
is the top singular vector of the population version of
\bm{T}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau}
(with respect to the missing data and noise). In addition, the spectral gap is large enough to guarantee accurate estimates.

2.

Next, we prove that given this spectral gap, the top singular vector
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
of
\bm{T}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau}
is close to
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{i}
both in the
\|\cdot\|_{2}
and
\|\cdot\|_{\infty}
norm. This also enables us to accurately estimate the magnitude of
\bm{u}^{\star}_{i}
.

3.

Finally, we need to show that one can find those reliable estimates among
L
random restarts. Combining the spectral gap information with the incoherence condition that tensor components are nearly orthogonal to each other, our selection procedure is guaranteed to recover all tensor factors.

Now we proceed to the proof. Without loss of generality, we prove the case for
i=1
in the sequel, i.e. there exists some
\tau\in\left[L\right]
such that
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
accurately recovers
\bm{u}^{\star}_{1}
. Together with the union bound, this shows that we can find reliable estimates
for all tensor factors. We then conclude the proof by showing that Algorithm
3
is able to find all of them without duplicates.

To this end, we find it convenient to introduce an auxiliary vector
\bm{\gamma}^{\star\tau}=\left[\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau},\cdots,\gamma_{r}^{\star\tau}\right]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
and its leave-one-out versions
\bm{\gamma}^{\star\tau,\left(m\right)}=\big[\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau,\left(m\right)},\cdots,\gamma_{r}^{\star\tau,\left(m\right)}\big]^{\top}
(
1\leq m\leq d
) for each
1\leq\tau\leq L
as follows:

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau}

\displaystyle:=\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\,\big\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau}\big\rangle=\lambda_{i}^{\star}\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau}\big\rangle,

(72a)

\displaystyle\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau,(m)}

\displaystyle:=\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\,\big\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau,(m)}\big\rangle=\lambda_{i}^{\star}\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau,(m)}\big\rangle,

(72b)

where
\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}
and
\lambda_{i}^{\star}
are both defined in (
66
).
The idea is to let
\bm{\gamma}^{\star\tau}

approximate the singular values of
\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau}
; this can be seen, for instance, via the following calculation:

\displaystyle\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\big\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau}\big\rangle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star\top}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\underset{=\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau}}{\underbrace{\lambda_{i}^{\star}\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau}\big\rangle}}\,\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star\top},

(73)

where
\{\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\}_{i=1}^{r}
— which are assumed to be incoherent (or nearly orthogonal to each other) — can be approximately viewed as the singular vectors of
\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau}
.

If we want our spectral estimate to be accurate, we would need to be assured that
the two largest entries of
\bm{\gamma}^{\star\tau}
(in magnitude) are sufficiently separated.

Lemma 5.12
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
5.9
. Define
\Delta_{1}^{\tau}:=\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\max_{1<i\leq r}\big|\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau}\big|
for each
1\leq\tau\leq L
and let
\Delta_{1}^{\left(1\right)}\geq\Delta_{1}^{\left(2\right)}\geq\dots\geq\Delta_{1}^{\left(L\right)}
denote the order statistics of
\big\{\Delta_{1}^{\tau}\big\}_{\tau=1}^{L}
(in descending order). Fix any arbitrary small constant
\delta>0
.
With probability greater than
1-\delta/r
, one has

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}^{\left(1\right)}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star},

(74a)

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}^{\left(1\right)}-\Delta_{1}^{\left(2\right)}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}{r\sqrt{\log d}},

(74b)

Additionally, for any
fixed vector
\bm{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
, with probability at least

1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, for all
1\leq\tau\leq L
, one has

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\log d}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

(75a)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{\gamma}^{\star\tau}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{r\log d}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

(75b)

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\bm{v},\bm{\gamma}^{\star\tau}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|\bm{v}\right\|_{2}\sqrt{\log d}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}.

(75c)

Proof.

See Appendix
B.1
. ∎

Lemma
5.12
demonstrates that there exists some
\tau\in\left[L\right]
such that
\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\max_{1<i\leq r}\big|\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau}\big|\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}
. This means that
\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}
exhibits the largest correlation with the random projection
\theta
, which further implies that
\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}
is the largest singular vector of
\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau}
with a considerable spectral gap (as we will show shortly).
With the desired spectral gap in place, we are ready to look at the eigenvectors / singular vectors of interest. To this end, we find it convenient to introduce another auxiliary vector
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
, defined as
the leading singular vector of
\bm{M}^{\tau}
(cf. (
70c
)) obeying

\displaystyle\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau},\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{1}\big\rangle\geq 0.

(76)

The careful reader would immediately notice the similarity between
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
and
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
except for their global signs; namely, we determine the global sign of
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
based on the ground truth information (
76
), but pick the global sign for
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
solely based on the observed data (cf. Algorithm
3
). Fortunately, the vectors
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
and
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
provably coincide, namely,

\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}=\bm{\nu}^{\tau},

(77)

as we shall demonstrate momentarily in Lemma
5.16
. In a similar way, we also denote by
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
the leading singular vector of
\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
defined in (
70d
) such that

\displaystyle\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)},\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{1}\big\rangle\geq 0.

(78)

Lemma
5.16
also shows that
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}=\bm{\nu}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
.

We shall now take a detour to look at
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
, which in turn would help us understand
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
. We shall first demonstrate that
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
(and hence
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
) is sufficiently close to the corresponding true factor in the
\ell_{2}
sense.

Lemma 5.13
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
5.9
. Let
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
and

\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}
be as defined in (
76
)
and (
66
), respectively. Define
\mathcal{A}

to be the event such that

\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\max_{1<i\leq r}\big|\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau}\big|\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}

and the condition (
75
) hold.
Then conditional on this
event
\mathcal{A}
, with probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)

one has

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\underbrace{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}}_{=:\,\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}}.

(79)

Proof.

See Appendix
B.2
. ∎

Thus far, we have focused on the
\ell_{2}
estimation errors. In order to further quantify the
\ell_{\infty}
estimation errors, we need to resort to the leave-one-out estimates
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
(
1\leq m\leq d
). Specifically, we shall justify in the following two lemmas that: (1) the
m
-th leave-one-out estimate
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
is close to the truth at least in the
m
-th coordinate, and (2) the vector
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
is extremely close to each of the leave-one-out estimates
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
(
1\leq m\leq d
). These two observations taken collectively translate to the desired entrywise error control of
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
. Here, we recall that the global sign of
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
(cf. (
78
))
and the global sign of
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
(cf. (
76
)) are defined in a similar fashion, both using the ground truth information.

Lemma 5.14
.

Instate the assumptions of Theorem
5.9
.
Define
\mathcal{A}
to be the event such that

\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\max_{1<i\leq r}\big|\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau}\big|\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}

and the condition (
75
) hold.
Then conditional on this
event
\mathcal{A}
, one has, with probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
,
that

\displaystyle\Big|\big[\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big]_{m}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}},

(80)

holds for all
m\in\left[d\right]
, where
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}:=\frac{\sqrt{\mu r}\,\log^{3}d}{d^{3/2}p}+\frac{\mu r\log^{5/2}d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\frac{\log^{7/2}d}{p}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{5}d}{p}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}.

(81)

Proof.

See Appendix
B.4
. ∎

Lemma 5.15
.

Instate the assumptions of Theorem
5.9
. Define
\mathcal{A}

to be the event such that

\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\max_{1<i\leq r}\big|\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau}\big|\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}

and the condition (
75
) hold.
Then conditional on this
event
\mathcal{A}
, one has, with probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, for all
m\in\left[d\right]
:

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}},

(82)

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\right)\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}},

(83)

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{\tau}-\lambda_{\tau}^{\left(m\right)}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

(84)

where
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}
and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}
are defined in (
81
) and (
65
), respectively.

Proof.

See Appendix
B.6
. ∎

Next, we turn to the estimation accuracy regarding the size of the tensor factors and show that
\lambda_{\tau}
(produced in Algorithm
3
) is close to the truth as well. As it turns out, a byproduct of this step reveals that
\bm{\nu}^{\tau}=\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
and
\bm{\nu}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}=\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
, where
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
and
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
are an auxiliary vectors defined in (
76
) and (
78
), respectively.

Lemma 5.16
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
5.9
. Assume that the results in
Lemma
5.13
, Lemma
5.14

and Lemma
5.15
hold. Then with probability
at least
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\big|\lambda_{\tau}-\lambda_{1}^{\star}\big|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\lambda_{1}^{\star}.

(85)

In particular, one has

\displaystyle\bm{\nu}^{\tau}=\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{\nu}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}=\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)},\quad 1\leq m\leq d.

(86)

Proof.

See Appendix
B.8
. ∎

Thus far, we have only proved that one can find a reliable estimate for each tensor factor within
L
random trials, provided that
L
is sufficiently large. To finish up, it remains to show that the pruning procedure
\textsc{Prune}(
) is capable of returning a rough estimate for each tensor factor without duplication. This is accomplished in the following lemma.

Lemma 5.17
.

Instate the assumptions
of Theorem
5.9
. On the event that the results in Lemma
5.13
, Lemma
5.14
, Lemma
5.15
and Lemma
5.16
hold for all
1\leq i\leq r
, there exists a permutation
\pi(\cdot):[d]\mapsto[d]
such that: for each
1\leq i\leq r
,
\big(\lambda_{i},\bm{w}^{i}\big)
and
\big(\lambda_{\pi(i)}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{\pi(i)}\big)
satisfy (
68a
), (
68b
) and (
68c
);
\big(\lambda_{i},\bm{w}^{i}\big)
and
\big(\lambda_{i}^{\left(m\right)},\bm{w}^{i,\left(m\right)}\big)_{i=1}^{r}
obey (
69a
), (
69b
) and (
69c
) for all
1\leq m\leq d
, where
\big\{\lambda_{i},\bm{w}^{i}\big\}_{i=1}^{r}
and
\big\{\lambda_{i}^{\left(m\right)},\bm{w}^{i,\left(m\right)}\big\}_{i=1}^{r}
are outputs of Algorithm
3
and Algorithm
6
, respectively.

Proof.

See Appendix
B.9
.
∎

6
Discussion

The current paper uncovers the possibility of efficiently and stably completing a low-CP-rank tensor from partial and noisy entries. Perhaps somewhat unexpectedly, despite the high degree of nonconvexity, this problem can be solved to optimal statistical accuracy within nearly linear time, provided that the tensor of interest is well-conditioned, incoherent, and of constant rank. To the best of our knowledge, this intriguing message has not been shown in the prior literature.

Moving forward, one pressing issue is to understand how to improve the algorithmic and theoretical dependency upon the tensor rank
r
of the proposed method. Ideally one would desire a fast algorithm whose sample complexity scales as
rd^{1.5}
, an order that is provably achievable by the sum-of-squares hierarchy. Additionally, in contrast to the matrix counterpart where the rank is upper bounded by the matrix dimension, the tensor CP rank is allowed to rise above
d
, which is commonly referred to as the over-complete case. Unfortunately, our current initialization scheme (i.e. the spectral method) fails to work unless
r<d
, and our local analysis for GD falls of accommodating the scenario with
r>d
. It would be of great interest to develop more powerful algorithms — in addition to more refined analysis — to tackle such an important over-complete regime.

Another tantalizing research direction is the exploration of landscape design for tensor completion. As our heuristic discussions as well as other prior work (e.g.
[
RM14
]
) suggest, randomly initialized gradient descent tailored to (
4
) seems unlikely to work, unless the sample size is significantly larger than the computational limit. This might mean either that there exist spurious local minima in the natural nonconvex least squares formulation (
4
), or that the optimization landscape of (
4
) is too flat around some saddle points and hence not amenable to fast computation. It would be interesting to investigate what families of loss functions allow us to rule out bad local minima and eliminate the need of careful initialization, which might be better suited for tensor recovery problems.

Finally, in statistical inference and decision making, one might not be simply satisfied with obtaining a reliable estimate for each missing entry, but would also like to report a short confidence interval which is likely to contain the true entry. This boils down to the fundamental task of uncertainty quantification for tensor completion, which we leave to future investigation.

Appendix A
Proofs for local convergence of GD

In this section, we establish the key lemmas concerning the convergence properties of GD. As one can easily see, treating
\{E_{i,j,k}\}_{1\leq i,j,k\leq d}
(resp.
\{\chi_{i,j,k}\}_{1\leq i,j,k\leq d}
) as independent random variables — which leads to asymmetric versions of
\bm{E}
and
\Omega
— does not affect the order of our results at all. In light of this, we shall adopt such an independent assumption whenever it simplifies our presentation.

A.1
Proof of Lemma
5.1

For notational convenience, for any matrix
\bm{M}=\left[\bm{m}_{1},\dots,\bm{m}_{r}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
, let

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{M}}:=\left[\bm{m}_{1}\otimes\bm{m}_{1},\dots,\bm{m}_{r}\otimes\bm{m}_{r}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times r},

(87)

where for any
\bm{a},\bm{b}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we denote
\bm{a}\otimes\bm{b}:={\footnotesize\left[\begin{array}[]{c}a_{1}\bm{b}\\
\vdots\\
a_{d}\bm{b}\end{array}\right]}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
.

From the Hessian expression (
41
), one can decompose

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}(\bm{V})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f(\bm{U})\,\mathsf{vec}(\bm{V})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{3p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}+\bm{u}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\big)\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{3p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\big)\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{=:\,\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{3p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\big)\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{3}\,\Big\|\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{=:\,\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\underbrace{\Big\langle\frac{1}{p}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big),\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}\Big\rangle}_{=:\,\alpha_{3}}+\underbrace{\frac{1}{3}\,\Big\|\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{=:\,\alpha_{4}}.

In what follows, we shall bound each of the above terms separately.

A.1.1
Bounding
\alpha_{4}

With regards to
\alpha_{4}
, by symmetry we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{4}=\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\,\Big\langle\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2},\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big\rangle.

(88)

In order to control (
88
), we first see that

\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\big\|\bm{V}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star\top}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}},

(89)

where
\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star}
is as defined in (
87
). Similar to the proof of Lemma
D.1
, we can use the fact that
\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star\otimes 2},\bm{u}_{j}^{\star\otimes 2}\rangle=\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{u}_{j}^{\star}\rangle^{2}
and (
8c
) to deduce that

\displaystyle\sigma_{\min}\big(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big)=\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}\left(1+o\left(1\right)\right)\qquad\text{and}\qquad\mathrm{\sigma}_{\max}\big(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big)=\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}\left(1+o\left(1\right)\right),

(90)

provided that
r\ll d/\mu
.
This implies that

\displaystyle\frac{19}{20}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sigma_{\min}\big(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big)\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\big\|\bm{V}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star\top}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sigma_{\max}\big(\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big)\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{11}{10}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

(91)

(1) Speaking of an upper bound on
\alpha_{4}
,
we can invoke the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality followed by (
91
) to reach

\displaystyle\alpha_{4}\leq 3\,\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=3\,\big\|\bm{V}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star\top}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\frac{7}{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(92)

(2) When it comes to lower bounding
\alpha_{4}
, the main step boils down to controlling the inner product term in (
88
).
Applying the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality gives that

\displaystyle\Big\langle\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2},\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in[r]}\left\langle\bm{v}_{s},\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\rangle^{2}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{s_{1}\neq s_{2}}\left\langle\bm{v}_{s_{1}},\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{2}}\right\rangle\left\langle\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{1}},\bm{v}_{s_{2}}\right\rangle\left\langle\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{1}},\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{2}}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\geq-\max_{s_{1}\neq s_{2}}\left|\left\langle\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{1}},\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{2}}\right\rangle\right|\sum_{s_{1}\neq s_{2}}\left\|\bm{v}_{s_{1}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{1}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\bm{v}_{s_{2}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{2}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq-\max_{s_{1}\neq s_{2}}\left|\left\langle\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{1}},\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{2}}\right\rangle\right|\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\left\|\bm{v}_{s}\right\|_{2}\left\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\right\|_{2}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\geq}-\max_{s_{1}\neq s_{2}}\left|\left\langle\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{1}},\bm{u}^{\star}_{s_{2}}\right\rangle\right|\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq-r\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq-\frac{1}{40}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

where (i) comes from Cauchy-Schwarz, and the last line follows from (
8c
), (
8d
) as well as the condition that
r\ll\sqrt{d/\mu}
and
\kappa\asymp 1
.
Therefore, we can lower bound
\alpha_{4}
by (with the assistance of (
91
))

\displaystyle\alpha_{4}

\displaystyle=\,\Big\|\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+2\,\Big\langle\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2},\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\frac{19}{20}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\frac{1}{20}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\penalty\ \geq\penalty\ \frac{9}{10}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(93)

A.1.2
Bounding
\alpha_{1}

When it comes to
\alpha_{1}
, we can expand

\displaystyle\Big\|

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=3\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+6\,\Big\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\rangle;

we can decompose
\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\big)\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
in a similar way.
As a consequence,

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{2}{p}\Bigg(\Big\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\rangle-\Big\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\rangle\Bigg)}_{=:\,\beta_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{p}\Bigg(\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Bigg)}_{=:\,\beta_{2}}.

We will derive an upper bound on
\beta_{1}
in the sequel; the same method immediately applies to
\beta_{2}
.

For notational convenience, let us define

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}:=\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star},\quad\bm{\Delta}_{s}:=\bm{u}_{s}-\bm{u}_{s}^{\star},\quad\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}:=[\bm{\Delta}_{1}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{1},\cdots,\bm{\Delta}_{r}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{r}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times r}.

(94)

Then one can write

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\beta_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\,\Big\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes(\bm{\Delta}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star})^{\otimes 2}\Big),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}(\bm{\Delta}_{s}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\rangle-\frac{1}{p}\,\Big\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\,\Big\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big),\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{1}{p}\,\Big\langle\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}^{\otimes 2}_{s}\Big),

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)+\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\rangle.

Apply the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality to yield that

\displaystyle\left|\beta_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big(2\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{p}\Big(\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+2\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\cdot\Big(2\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}^{\otimes 2}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\Big).

(95)

Before we bound the above quantities, we pause to make the following observations. In view of the assumptions of this lemma that
\delta\ll 1/\sqrt{r}\leq 1
, the following holds for all
i\in\left[r\right]
:

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\sqrt{r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\ll\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(96a)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\delta\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\ll\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d}}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(96b)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(96c)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{u}_{i}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}+\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\right\|_{\infty}\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(96d)

Consequently, we also know that

\displaystyle\big\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}\big\|_{2,\infty}\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq r}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\delta^{2}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}.

(97)

Now, we proceed to prove the claim. Let us define
\mathcal{S}_{i}:=\{\underline{j}\in[d]^{2}\mid\chi_{ij_{1}j_{2}}=1\}
for each
i\in[d]
. Applying the Chernoff bound and the union bound yields that: with probability at least
1-O(d^{-10})
one has

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\left[d\right]}|\mathcal{S}_{i}|\lesssim d^{2}p.

(98)

provided
p\gg d^{-2}\log d
. It then follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in[d],\underline{j}\in[d]^{2}}\chi_{ij_{1}j_{2}}\big\langle\bm{V}_{i,:},\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}_{\underline{j},:}\big\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i\in\left[d\right]}\big\|\bm{V}_{i,:}\big\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{\underline{j}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}}\big\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}_{\underline{j},:}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p}\max_{i\in[d]}\left|\mathcal{S}_{i}\right|\big\|\widetilde{\bm{\Delta}}\big\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(i)}}{\lesssim}d^{2}\delta^{4}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{4}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\delta^{4}\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

where (i) arises from (
97
) and (
98
).
In a similar manner, we can derive

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\max_{1\leq i\leq d}\left|\mathcal{S}_{i}\right|\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim d^{2}\delta^{2}\max_{1\leq i\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}\mu^{2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Regarding
p^{-1}\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\big)\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}
, we apply
[
YZ16
, Lemma 5]
(with slight modification, which we omit here for brevity) to show that: with probability exceeding
1-O(d^{-}{10})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{p}}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{3}{2}\,\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\frac{3}{2}\big\|\bm{V}\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star\top}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{3}{2}\,\big\|\widetilde{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big\|\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

under the sample size assumptin that
p\gg\mu^{2}r^{2}d^{-2}\log d
. Here the last inequality makes use of (
90
).
It is self-evident that the above bounds also hold for quantities that appear in (
95
). Since
0<\delta\ll 1/\sqrt{r}<1
, we obtain

\displaystyle|\beta_{1}|\lesssim\delta\mu\sqrt{r}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

The same upper bound holds for any other
\beta_{2}
. Therefore, as long as
0<\delta\ll 1/(\mu\sqrt{r})
and
\kappa\asymp 1
, we have

\displaystyle|\alpha_{1}|\leq\frac{1}{10}\lambda^{\star 4/3}_{\min}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(99)

A.1.3
Bounding
\alpha_{2}

Regarding
\alpha_{2}
, applying
[
YZ16
, Lemma 5]
(with slight modification, which we omit here for brevity) implies that: if
p\gg\mu^{2}r^{2}d^{-2}\log d
, then with probability exceeding
1-O(d^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\Bigg|\frac{1}{2p}

\displaystyle\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}(\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s})\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\,\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}(\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s})\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{100}\,\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}(\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{v}_{s})\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\frac{1}{100}\alpha_{4}\leq\frac{1}{10}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Here the last inequality arises from (
92
).

A.1.4
Bounding
\alpha_{3}

We now move on to bounding
\alpha_{3}
. The triangle inequality gives

\displaystyle\Big|\Big\langle p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big),\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\bm{v}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\in[r]}\Big|\Big\langle p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\times_{3}\bm{u}_{s},\bm{v}_{s}\bm{v}_{s}^{\top}\Big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{s\in\left[r\right]}\Big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\times_{3}\bm{u}_{s}\Big\|\sum_{s\in[r]}\left\|\bm{v}_{s}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{s\in\left[r\right]}\Big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\times_{3}\bm{u}_{s}\Big\|\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

Recall the definitions of
\bm{\Delta}
and
\bm{\Delta}_{i}
in (
94
). Fix an arbitrary
s\in\left[r\right]
. From the definition of the operator norm and the triangle inequality, we can derive

\displaystyle\Big\|\,p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\times_{3}\bm{u}_{s}\Big\|\leq\left\|\bm{u}_{s}\right\|_{2}\Big\|\,p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big\|.

(100)

In order to upper bound
\big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\big\|
as required in (
100
), we invoke the following simple fact, which follows immediately from the definition of the operator norm. Here and throughout, for any tensor
\bm{A}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}
we denote

|\bm{A}|:=\big[|A_{i,j,k}|\big]_{1\leq i,j,k\leq d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}.

Lemma A.1
.

Consider any tensor
\bm{A},\bm{B}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}
obeying
|B_{i,j,k}|\geq|A_{i,j,k}|
for all
1\leq i,j,k\leq d
. One has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}\right\|\leq\big\||\bm{A}|\big\|\leq\big\||\bm{B}|\big\|.

(101)

With this lemma in mind, we are ready to derive that

\displaystyle\Big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\Big|\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big|\Big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big\|_{\infty}\left\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\bm{1}^{\otimes 3}\big)\right\|.

Here,
\bm{1}
stands for the all-one vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
This suggests that we shall upper bound
\left\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\bm{1}^{\otimes 3}\big)\right\|
and
\big\|\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}
.

Given that
p\gtrsim d^{-3/2}\log^{3}d
, applying Lemma
D.2
indicates that with probability at least
1-O(d^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\left\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\bm{1}^{\otimes 3}\big)-\bm{1}^{\otimes 3}\right\|\lesssim\frac{\log^{3}d}{p}+\sqrt{\frac{d\log^{5}d}{p}}\lesssim d^{3/2}.

(102)

Moreover, it is straightforward to see that

\left\|\bm{1}^{\otimes 3}\right\|=\left\|\bm{1}\right\|_{2}^{3}=d^{3/2}
. Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\left\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\bm{1}^{\otimes 3}\big)\right\|\lesssim d^{3/2}.

(103)

Next, we turn to
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\infty}
. We first expand

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}^{\otimes 3}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}&=\sum_{i\in[r]}\big((\bm{\Delta}_{i}+\bm{u}^{\star}_{i})^{\otimes 3}-\bm{u}_{i}^{\star\otimes 3}\big)=\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{i}\otimes\bm{u}^{\star\otimes 2}_{i}+\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}^{\star}_{i}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{i}\otimes\bm{u}^{\star}_{i}+\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}^{\star\otimes 2}_{i}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{i}\\
&\quad+\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{u}^{\star}_{i}+\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{i}\otimes\bm{u}^{\star}_{i}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{i}+\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}^{\star}_{i}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\otimes 2}+\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\otimes 3}.\end{split}

By symmetry, it suffices to control

\big\|\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}^{\star\otimes 2}_{i}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{i}\big\|
,

\big\|\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{u}^{\star}_{i}\big\|

and

\big\|\sum_{i\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\otimes 3}\big\|
.
Let us look at the first term. Towards this, for each
\left(i,j,k\right)\in\left[d\right]^{3}
, we can use the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality to control

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\Big)_{i,j,k}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\left(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right)_{i}\left(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right)_{j}\left(\bm{\Delta}_{s}\right)_{k}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\big[\left(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right)_{i}\big]^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\big[\left(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right)_{j}\big]^{2}\big[\left(\bm{\Delta}_{s}\right)_{k}\big]^{2}\Big)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{2,\infty}\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty},

which implies that

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\Big\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\delta\mu^{3/2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}}.

(104)

In a similar manner, we can control the remaining two terms by

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{u}^{\star}_{i}\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\delta^{2}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}\mu^{3/2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}};

(105)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\otimes 3}\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{s}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\delta^{3}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{3}\leq\frac{\delta^{3}\mu^{3/2}r^{3/2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}}.

(106)

Recall that
0<\delta\ll 1/r\leq 1
. Putting these together reveals that

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}^{\otimes 3}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big\|\lesssim\frac{\delta\mu^{3/2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}}.

(107)

This combined with (
103
) yields

\displaystyle\Big\|\,p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}^{\otimes 3}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big\|\lesssim\delta\mu^{3/2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

thus indicating that

\displaystyle\Big\|\,p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\times_{3}\bm{u}_{s}\Big\|\leq\left\|\bm{u}_{s}\right\|_{2}\Big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}^{\otimes 3}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big\|\lesssim\delta\mu^{3/2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3},

(108)

where we use (
96c
) in the last step.
In view of the condition that
\delta\ll 1/(\mu^{3/2}r)
and the assumption
\kappa\asymp 1
, one has with probability greater than
1-O(d^{-10})
,

\displaystyle|\alpha_{3}|\leq\frac{1}{10}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(109)

A.1.5
Putting all this together

Note that the above bounds hold uniformly for all
\bm{V}
. Therefore,
combining upper bounds for
\alpha_{i}
and the union bound, we conclude that with probability exceeding
1-O(d^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}(\bm{V})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f(\bm{U})\mathsf{vec}(\bm{V})

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{4}-|\alpha_{1}|-|\alpha_{2}|-|\alpha_{3}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(110)

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}(\bm{V})^{\top}\nabla^{2}f(\bm{U})\mathsf{vec}(\bm{V})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{4}+|\alpha_{1}|+|\alpha_{2}|+|\alpha_{3}|\leq 4\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{V}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

(111)

as claimed.

A.2
Proof of Lemma
5.2

From (
47a
) and (
47c
), we use the triangle inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2C_{1}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+2C_{2}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

Similarly, we can combine (
47b
) and (
47c
) to obtain that

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{2,\infty}+\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(C_{5}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{6}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\left(C_{3}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{4}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\left(C_{3}+C_{5}\right)\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+\left(C_{4}+C_{6}\right)\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}.

A.3
Proof of Lemma
5.3

In view of the relation (
40
), one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{U}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\left(\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t}\times_{2}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{=:\,\alpha_{1}}+\underbrace{\eta\left\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t}\times_{2}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}}_{=:\,\alpha_{2}},

which motivates us to bound
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
separately.

(1) We start with
\alpha_{1}
, towards which we find it helpful to define

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{t}(\tau):=\tau\,\bm{U}^{t}+\left(1-\tau\right)\bm{U}^{\star}.

(112)

Given that
\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{\star}\right)=\bm{0}
(since
\bm{U}^{\star}
is a global optimizer of
f_{\mathsf{clean}}
),
we can use the fundamental theorem of calculus to obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\bm{U}^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\left(\bm{U}^{\star}-\eta\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{\star}\right)\right)\right)

(113)

\displaystyle=\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right)-\eta\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{\star}\right)\right)

(114)

\displaystyle=\Bigg(\bm{I}_{dr}-\eta\underset{=:\,\bm{\Gamma}}{\underbrace{\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\left(\tau\right)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\tau}}\Bigg)\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right).

(115)

It then follows that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}=\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right)^{\top}\left(\bm{I}_{dr}-\eta\bm{\Gamma}\right)^{2}\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}-2\eta\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right)^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right)+\eta^{2}\left\|\bm{\Gamma}\right\|^{2}\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}.

(116)

From the hypothesis (
47b
) as well as our conditions that
\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}\ll\frac{1}{\mu^{3/2}r}
, we know that
\bm{U}^{t}(\tau)
(
0\leq\tau\leq 1
) satisfies the conditions required in Lemma
5.1
. Therefore, applying Lemma
5.1
gives that

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right)^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\Gamma}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 4\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}.

Substitution into (
116
) indicates that: if
0<\eta\leq\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}/\big(32\lambda_{\max}^{\star 8/3}\big)
, then

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\big(1-\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta+16\lambda_{\max}^{\star 8/3}\eta^{2}\big)\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\big(1-\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\big)\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

which implies that (since
1-a/2\geq\sqrt{1-a}
for
0<a<1
)

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\big(1-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\big)\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

(117)

(2) We now turn to
\alpha_{2}
. To simplify presentation, we shall assume that
\{E_{i,j,k}\}_{i,j,k\in[d]}
(resp.
\{\chi_{i,j,k}\}_{i,j,k\in[d]}
) are independent random variables.
Fix an arbitrary
s\in[r]
and
m\in[d]
. The
m
-th entry of
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\times_{2}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}
can be expanded as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\times_{2}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\right)_{m}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\bm{u}^{t\top}_{s}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\left|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)\top}_{s}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\right|}_{=:\,\beta_{1}}+\underbrace{\left|\big(\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)^{\top}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\right|}_{=:\,\beta_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left|\bm{u}^{t\top}_{s}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\big(\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)\right|}_{=:\,\beta_{3}}+\underbrace{\left|\big(\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)^{\top}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\big(\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)\right|}_{=:\,\beta_{4}}.

(118)

Before continuing, we make the following observations: from the hypotheses (
47a
), (
47b
), (
47c
), as well as our assumption that
\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}\ll\frac{1}{\mu^{3/2}r}
, we see that the following holds for all
s\in\left[r\right]
:

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\ll\frac{1}{r}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(119a)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\ll\frac{1}{r}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{rd}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(119b)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}-\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\ll\frac{1}{r}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{rd}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(119c)

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}-\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big)_{m}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big)_{m,:}\big\|_{2}\ll\frac{1}{r}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{rd}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

(119d)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}-\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\ll\frac{1}{r}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{r}}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(119e)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{1/3},

(119f)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{1/3},

(119g)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}-\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

(119h)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}-\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{1/3},

(119i)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\big\|\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}},

(119j)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}+\big\|\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\big\|\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty},

(119k)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\big\|\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}},

(119l)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\big\|\bm{U}^{t}\big\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\big\|\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}.

(119m)

With these estimates in place, we can upper bound the above four terms in (
118
) separately.

•

For
\beta_{1}
, we note that, by construction,
\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}
is independent of the
m
-th mode-3 slice of
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)
. This tells us that

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)\top}_{s}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}=\sum\nolimits_{i,j\in[d]}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{i}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}E_{i,j,m}\chi_{i,j,m}

can be viewed as a sum of independent zero-mean random variables (conditional on
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{-m}}\left(\bm{E}\right)
).
It is straightforward to compute that

\displaystyle\max_{i,j\in[d]}\left\|\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{i}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}E_{i,j,m}\chi_{i,j,m}\right\|_{\psi_{1}}\lesssim\sigma\,\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}=:L,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\left[\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{i}^{2}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}^{2}E_{i,j,m}^{2}\chi_{i,j,m}^{2}\right]\lesssim\sigma^{2}p\,\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{2}^{4}\lesssim\sigma^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}p\,\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{2}^{2}=:V,

where
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
denotes the sub-exponential norm and we use (
119
) in the last inequality.
Applying the matrix Bernstein inequality
[
Kol11
, Corollary 2.1]
yields that: with probability
1-O(d^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\left|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)\top}_{s}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim L\log^{2}d+\sqrt{V\log d}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\log^{2}d\,\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}+\sigma\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\sqrt{p\log d}\,\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{2},

(120)

•

For
\beta_{2}
, we first invoke
[
CW15
, lemma 11]
to demonstrate that: with probability at least
1-O(d^{-11})
,

\displaystyle\max_{m\in[d]}\big\|\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\big\|\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{dp}+\log d\big).

(121)

Next, it is seen that

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\bm{u}^{t}_{s}

\displaystyle-\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)^{\top}\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\Big|\leq\big\|\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\right)_{:,:,m}\big\|\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}-\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{2}\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{dp}+\log d\big)\big\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\big(\sqrt{dp}+\log d\big)\left(C_{5}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{6}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\ll\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}}{\sqrt{d}}\left(C_{5}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{6}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2},

where the last inequality follows from the noise condition. Clearly, the above bound holds for
\beta_{3}
as well.

•

Regarding
\beta_{4}
, it is easily seen that
\beta_{4}\ll\beta_{2}
, given that
\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\ll\|\bm{U}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}/r\leq\lambda_{\min}^{\star}
holds according to (
119
) and
\kappa\asymp 1
.

•

Taking together the above bounds and substituting them into (
118
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\left(\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\times_{2}\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\right)_{m}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\log^{2}d\,\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}+\sigma\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\sqrt{p\log d}\,\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\quad+o\left(1\right)\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}}{\sqrt{d}}\left(C_{5}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{6}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\big\|\bm{u}^{t}_{s}\big\|_{2}.

Recognizing that this holds for any
m\in[d]
and
s\in[r]
, one can sum over
m
and
s
to deduce that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t}\times_{2}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\lesssim}\frac{\sigma\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}\mu\sqrt{r}\log^{2}d}{\sqrt{d}}+\sigma\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\sqrt{dp\log d}\,\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+o\left(1\right)\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(C_{5}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{6}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{t}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sigma\lambda_{\min}^{\star 1/3}\sqrt{dp\log d}\,\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\quad+o\left(1\right)\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(C_{5}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{6}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

(122)

for some absolute constant
C>0
, where (i) is true due to (
119
); the last inequality follows from the fact that
\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\lambda_{\min}^{\star 1/3}\sqrt{r}
as well as the assumptions that
p\gg\mu d^{-2}\log^{3}d
and
\kappa\asymp 1
.

(3) Combining (
117
) and (
122
) yields that: with probability at least
1-O(d^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{U}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\big)\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+C\eta\sigma\lambda_{\min}^{\star 1/3}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+o\left(1\right)\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\left(C_{5}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{6}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\big)\left(C_{1}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{2}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+C\eta\sigma\lambda_{\min}^{\star 1/3}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\qquad+o\left(1\right)\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\left(C_{5}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}+C_{6}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\frac{1}{5}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\big)C_{1}\rho^{t}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+\left(\big(1-\frac{1}{5}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\big)C_{2}+C\eta\lambda_{\min}^{\star 3/4}\right)\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\rho^{t+1}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{local}}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}+C_{2}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

with the proviso that
0<\eta\leq\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}/\big(32\lambda_{\max}^{\star 8/3}\big)
,
1-\big(\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}/5\big)\eta\leq\rho<1
and that
C_{2}
is sufficiently large.

A.4
Proof of Lemma
5.4

Fix an arbitrary
m\in[r]
. From the definition of
f^{\left(m\right)}
in (
45
) and (
46
), we can show that

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{t+1}-\bm{U}^{t+1,\left(m\right)}=\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\nabla f\big(\bm{U}^{t}\big)-\Big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\eta\nabla f^{\left(m\right)}\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad=\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\eta\left(\nabla f\big(\bm{U}^{t}\big)-\nabla f\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big)\right)-\eta\left(\nabla f\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big)-\nabla f^{\left(m\right)}\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big)\right)

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\eta\left(\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\big(\bm{U}^{t}\big)-\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big)\right)}_{=:\,\alpha_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\underbrace{\eta\,\Big(\left(p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{m}}-\mathcal{P}_{m}\right)\Big(\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big)\times_{1}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\times_{2}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}}_{=:\,\alpha_{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\eta\,p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{m}}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\times_{2}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}}_{=:\,\alpha_{3}}+\underbrace{\eta p^{-1}\,\big\{\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t}\times_{2}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t}-\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)\times_{1}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\times_{2}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\}}_{=:\,\alpha_{4}}.

(123)

Before proceeding to bounding these terms, we pause to define

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}:=\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)^{\otimes 3}-\bm{T}^{\star}.

(124)

From (
119
) and hypothesis (
47d
), one can applying a similar argument as in (
107
) to find that

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\max_{s\in[r]}\big\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}\big\|\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}\lesssim\frac{\mu\sqrt{r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}}{d}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}.

(125)

Now we begin to bound the terms in (
123
) separately.

(1) We start with
\alpha_{1}
. For any
0\leq\tau\leq 1
, define

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}(\tau):=\tau\,\bm{U}^{t}+\left(1-\tau\right)\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}.

The fundamental theorem of calculus yields

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\eta\left(\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\big(\bm{U}^{t}\big)-\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big)\right)=\underbrace{\Big(\bm{I}_{dr}-\eta\int_{0}^{1}\nabla^{2}f_{\mathsf{clean}}\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\left(\tau\right)\big)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big)}_{=:\bm{\Gamma}}\big(\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big).

By Lemma
5.2
and our assumptions on the noise, we know that
\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}(\tau)
(
0\leq\tau\leq 1
) satisfies the conditions in Lemma
5.1
for any
\tau\in[0,1]
.
Applying the same argument as the one used to bound
\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\eta\nabla f_{\mathsf{clean}}\left(\bm{U}^{t}\right)-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
in Lemma
5.3
, we show that

\displaystyle\Big\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\eta\Big(\nabla f\big(\bm{U}^{t}\big)-\nabla f\big(\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big)\Big)\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(1-\frac{1}{4}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\eta\big)\big\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}},

(126)

provided that
0<\eta\leq\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}/\big(32\lambda_{\max}^{\star 8/3}\big)
.

In what follows, we shall assume that
\{E_{i,j,k}\}_{i,j,k\in[d]}
(resp.
\{\chi_{i,j,k}\}_{i,j,k\in[d]}
) are independent random variables in order to simplify presentation.

(2) The next step is to bound
\alpha_{2}
.
For notational simplicity, define

\displaystyle\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\left(p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{m}}-\mathcal{P}_{m}\right)\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)\Big)\times_{1}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\times_{2}^{\mathsf{seq}}\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)};

\displaystyle\bm{v}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}

\displaystyle:=\Big(\left(p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{m}}-\mathcal{P}_{m}\right)\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)\Big)\times_{1}\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\times_{2}\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s},\qquad s\in[r].

In order to control the Frobenius norm of
\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}
, we shall start by considering the
m
-th row of
\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}
. In view of the definitions of
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{m}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{m}
(cf. Appendix
5.1.3
), we can expand

\displaystyle\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{m,:}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\sum_{s\in[r]}\left(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,m}-1\right)\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)_{i,j,m}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{i}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}\bm{e}_{s}^{\top}.

We recognize that
\big\{\bm{u}_{s}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\}_{s=1}^{r}
is independent of
\Omega_{m}
, making it convenient for us to upper bound
\big\|\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{m,:}\big\|_{2}
. Specifically, for any
i,j\in[d]
, from (
119
) and (
125
) we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)_{i,j,m}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{i}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}\left(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,m}-1\right)\bm{e}_{s}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p}\,\big\|\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big\|_{\infty}\max_{s\in[r]}\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}}{d^{2}p}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}=:L_{1}.

In addition, it is easy to verify that
\mathbb{E}\big[\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{m,:}\big]=\bm{0}
and

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Bigg[\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)_{i,j,m}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{i}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,m}-1)\,\bm{e}_{s}^{\top}\Big\|_{2}^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)^{2}_{i,j,m}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{i}^{2}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\big[(p^{-1}{\chi}_{i,j,m}-1)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p}\,\big\|\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\max_{s\in[r]}\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{2}^{2}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 8/3}}{d^{2}p}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}^{2}=:V_{1},

where the last inequality holds due to (
119
) and (
125
). We then apply the matrix Bernstein inequality to yield that: with probability exceeding
1-O(d^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{m,:}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim L_{1}\log d+\sqrt{V_{1}\log d}\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu^{2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\log d}{d^{2}p}+\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\sqrt{\log d}}{d\sqrt{p}}\Bigg\}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\sqrt{\log d}}{d\sqrt{p}}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty},

(127)

where the last step holds as long as
p\gg\mu^{2}d^{-2}\log d
.

Next, we turn to the
k
-th row of
\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}
for any
k\neq m
. For each
s\in[r]
, we have

\displaystyle\big(\bm{v}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{k}

\displaystyle=\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{m}\sum_{j\in[d]}\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)_{m,j,k}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)

\displaystyle\quad+\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{m}\sum_{i:i\neq m}\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)_{i,m,k}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{i}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,m,k}-1).

Similar to the proof of Lemma
D.9
, we can show that with probability at least
1-O(d^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{s\in[r]}\sum_{k:k\neq m}

\displaystyle\Big(\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{m}\sum\nolimits_{j\in[d]}\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)_{m,j,k}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{s\in[r]}\sum_{k:k\neq m}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{m}\sum\nolimits_{j\in[d]}\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)_{m,j,k}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)\Big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\sum_{s\in[r]}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{m}^{2}\sum_{j,k\in[d]}\big(\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big)_{m,j,k}^{2}\big(\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{p}\max_{s\in[r]}\big\|\bm{u}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\big\|\bm{\Delta}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{\bm{T}}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu^{3}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 8/3}}{d^{2}p}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}^{2},

where the last inequality follows from (
119
), and (
125
). It is easily seen that the bound also holds for the summation over
i\neq m
. We can then use Cauchy-Schwartz to arrive at

\displaystyle\sum_{k:k\neq m}\big\|\bm{V}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{k,:}\big\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{s\in[r]}\sum_{k:k\neq m}\big(\bm{v}^{t,\left(m\right)}_{s}\big)_{k}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu^{3}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 8/3}}{d^{2}p}\big\|\bm{U}^{t,\left(m\right)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}^{2}.

(128)

Combining (
128
) with (
127
