\displaystyle\alpha_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}

where we remind the reader of the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}
in (
81
).

B.4.3
Controlling
\alpha_{1}

The remaining quantity to control is
\alpha_{1}
(see (
179
)).
Invoke Weyl’s inequality to show that

\displaystyle\big|\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\gamma_{\tau}^{(m)}\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\star\tau}\big\|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}\ll\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau},

where the last inequality arises from (
188
) and Lemma
5.12
.
Under our sample size, rank and noise conditions, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}\leq\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\big|\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\gamma_{\tau}^{(m)}\big|\leq\gamma_{\tau}^{(m)}\leq\big|\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\gamma_{\tau}^{(m)}\big|+\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}\leq 2\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}.

This indicates that

\displaystyle\frac{\big|\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\gamma_{\tau}^{(m)}\big|}{\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}\gamma_{\tau}^{(m)}}\lesssim\frac{1}{\big(\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\star\tau}\big\|\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}.

Moreover, we learn from (
195
) and (
181
) that

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{M}_{m,:}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\big(\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\star\tau}\big)_{m,:}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\bm{M}_{m,:}^{\star\tau}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu r\sqrt{\log d}}{d}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\asymp\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\min}^{\star},

where the last step follows from the fact that
\mu r\leq d
and
\kappa\asymp 1
. Hence,
we reach the conclusion that

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\big|\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}-\gamma_{\tau}^{(m)}\big|}{\gamma_{1}^{\star\tau}\gamma_{\tau}^{(m)}}\big\|\bm{M}_{m,:}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}.

B.4.4
Combining
\alpha_{1}
,
\alpha_{2}
and
\alpha_{3}

Putting together all of the preceding bounds on
\alpha_{1}
,
\alpha_{2}

and
\alpha_{3}
immediately establishes the lemma.

B.5
Proof of Lemma
B.2

First of all, if the claims (
185
) and (
186
) can be established, then putting them together yields

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|+\big\|\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\sqrt{\log d}}{d\sqrt{p}}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{rd\log d}{p}},

(196)

where we recall the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}
in (
65
) and use the sample size, noise and rank conditions. The rest of the proof is thus dedicated to establishing (
185
) and (
186
). In what follows, we shall assume
\{E_{i,j,k}\}_{i,j,k\in[d]}
(resp.
\{\chi_{i,j,k}\}_{i,j,k\in[d]}
) are independent random variables to simplify presentation.

B.5.1
Proximity of
\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
and
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

Recall the definition of
\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}=p^{-1}\bm{T}^{(m)}\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

in (
70c
). Comparing this with the definition of
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

in (
183
), we see that

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\theta_{m}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big(T_{i,j,m}^{\star}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,m}-1)+p^{-1}E_{i,j,m}\chi_{i,j,m}\big),\;i\neq m,j\neq m,

(197a)

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{i,m}

\displaystyle=\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}\big(T_{i,m,k}^{\star}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,m,k}-1)+p^{-1}E_{i,m,k}\chi_{i,m,k}\big),\;i\neq m,

(197b)

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{m,j}

\displaystyle=\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}\big(T_{m,j,k}^{\star}(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)+p^{-1}E_{m,j,k}\chi_{m,j,k}\big).

(197c)

Note that
\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\bm{0},\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)}\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)\top}\right)

conditional on
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)
. Standard Gaussian concentration inequalities reveal that with probability exceeding
1-O(d^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\sqrt{r\log d}.

(198)

From Lemmas
5.7
-
5.8
and the fact that
\max\{\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{se}},\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\}\ll 1
,
we have

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}\big(\theta_{i}^{\tau}\big)

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{U}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu r}{d}+\left\|\bm{U}_{\mathsf{orth}}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\asymp\frac{\mu r}{d},

\displaystyle\max_{i\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}\big(\theta_{i}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)

\displaystyle=\big\|\bm{U}^{(m)}\big\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\lesssim\left\|\bm{U}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu r}{d},\quad 1\leq m\leq d.

As a consequence, standard concentration results assert that with
probability
1-O(d^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\max_{i\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}\big(\theta_{i}^{\tau}\big)\log d}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}};

(199)

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\max_{i\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}\big(\theta_{i}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\log d}\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}},\quad 1\leq m\leq d.

(200)

•

Regarding the
m
-th row of
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
,
apply Lemma
D.9
to show that with
probability
1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)
,

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j\in[d]}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}T_{m,j,k}^{\star}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)\Big)^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log d}{d^{2}p},

where the last inequality comes from (
200
). In addition,
Lemma
D.10
indicates that with
probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)
,

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{j\in[d]}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}E_{m,j,k}\chi_{m,j,k}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}dp\,\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\log^{5}d

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}rdp\log d+\frac{\sigma^{2}\mu r\log^{6}d}{d}\asymp\sigma^{2}rdp\log d,

where the second line comes from (
200
) and (
198
),
and the last inequality holds as long as
p\gg\mu d^{-2}\log^{5}d
.
These together with (
197c
) allow us to obtain

\displaystyle\left\|\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{m,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log d}{d^{2}p}+\frac{\sigma^{2}rd\log d}{p}.

(201)

Clearly, this bound is also valid for
\sum_{i:i\neq m}\big\{\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{i,m}\big\}^{2}
,
namely,

\displaystyle\sum_{i:i\neq m}\left\{\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{i,m}\right\}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log d}{d^{2}p}+\frac{\sigma^{2}rd\log d}{p}.

(202)

•

When it comes to the remaining entries of
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
, by the fact that the spectral norm of a submatrix is always less than or equal to that of the whole matrix,
applying the matrix Bernstein inequality gives that with probability

1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)
,

\displaystyle\Big\|\Big[\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{i,j}\Big]_{i,j\neq m}\Big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}\left(\frac{\log d}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{p}}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{\sigma\log^{2}d}{p}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\mu r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\log d}{d^{3/2}p}+\frac{\mu\sqrt{r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\sqrt{\log d}}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma\log^{2}d}{p}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\sqrt{\log d}}{d\sqrt{p}}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{rd\log d}{p}},

as long as our sample size and rank condition holds.

•

Putting the preceding bounds together yields

\big\|\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\sqrt{\log d}}{d\sqrt{p}}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{rd\log d}{p}}.

(203)

B.5.2
Proximity of
\bm{M}^{\tau}
and
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

Recall the definitions of
\bm{M}^{\tau}
and
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

in (
279b
) and (
183
), respectively. From
the definition of the operator norm and the triangle inequality, we
have

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\big\|\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{3}\big(\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\big\|}_{=:\,\alpha_{1}}+\underbrace{\big\|\left(p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right)\times_{3}\big(\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\big\|}_{=:\,\alpha_{2}}.

(204)

•

To control
\alpha_{1}
, we can express

\displaystyle\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{3}\big(\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\left[r\right]}\lambda_{s}^{\star}\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\rangle\,\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star}\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star\top}.

(205)

As shown in (
193
),
with probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-12}\right)
,

\displaystyle\big|\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau}\big\rangle\big|\lesssim\big\|\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}-\bm{U}^{(m)}\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)\top}\big\|\sqrt{\log d}.

Consequently, we know from Lemma
5.8
that with probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
,

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{3}\big(\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{s\in[r]}\big|\lambda_{s}^{\star}\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\rangle\big|\big\|\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big\|^{2}\lesssim\big\|\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}-\bm{U}^{(m)}\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)\top}\big\|\sqrt{\log d}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

(206)

where we use the fact that
\big\|\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big\|\lesssim 1

if
r\sqrt{\mu/d}\ll 1
.

•

When it comes to
\alpha_{2}
, combining (
171
)
and (
194
) with our sample size, noise and rank conditions, one has

\displaystyle\big\|\left(p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right)\times_{3}\big(\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\big\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\ll\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau}-\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big\|\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}-\bm{U}^{(m)}\bm{U}^{\left(m\right)\top}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\sqrt{\log d}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}.

(207)

•

Combining (
204
), (
206
) and (
207
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}.

B.6
Proof of Lemma
5.15

•

We start with the first claim regarding
\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\bm{\nu}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\|_{2}
,
or equivalently,
\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\|_{2}
(as argued in the proof of Lemma
5.14
).
By the triangle inequality, we can upper bound the following two terms
separately:

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\leq\underbrace{\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}}_{=:\,\beta_{1}}+\underbrace{\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}}_{=:\,\beta_{2}}.

(208)

Here, we remind the reader that
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

is the top left singular vector of
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

(see (
183
)) obeying
\big\langle\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)},\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle\geq 0
.

–

The first term
\beta_{1}
shall be bounded via Wedin’s theorem.
From (
163
) and (
164
), we have

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau}\big)-\sigma_{2}(\bm{M}^{\tau})

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{1}(\bm{M}^{\star\tau})-\sigma_{2}(\bm{M}^{\star\tau})-2\,\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\bm{M}^{\star\tau}\big\|\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}.

(209)

Combined with Lemma
B.2
, one has

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau}\big)-\sigma_{2}(\bm{M}^{\tau})-\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}.

Note that we have already shown in the proof of Lemma
B.2

and Lemma
B.4
that
\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\|_{2}=o(1)

and
\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\|_{2}=o(1)
,
which implies that
\bm{u}^{\tau}
and
\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

are positively correlated.
Thus, one can invoke Wedin’s theorem and use
the bound (
186
) to reach

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|}{\sigma_{1}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau}\big)-\sigma_{2}(\bm{M}^{\tau})-\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|}\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{r\log d}\,\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\}.

(210)

In addition to this bound on
\beta_{1}
, we also make note of the
following simple bound

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\},

(211)

where the last inequality follows from our sample size, noise and rank condition
that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{r\log d}\ll 1
.

–

The second term
\beta_{2}
is also controlled via Wedin’s theorem:

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\leq\frac{\big\|\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}}{\sigma_{1}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)-\sigma_{2}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)-\big\|\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|}.

(212)

The denominator term is easy to handle. With (
196
) and (
209
)
in mind, we can apply Weyl’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)-\sigma_{2}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{1}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau}\big)-\sigma_{2}(\bm{M}^{\tau})-2\,\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}.

(213)

From Lemma
B.2
, one has
\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star}
.
Therefore, we know that

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)-\sigma_{2}\big(\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)-\big\|\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}.

In addition, Lemma
B.3
below develops an upper bound on the numerator term:

Lemma B.3
.

Instate the assumptions of Lemma
5.15
. With probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}\Bigg\}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}.

(214)

Proof.

See Appendix
B.7
.
∎

–

Substitution of the above bounds into (
212
) yields

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}\Bigg\}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}\ll\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty},

where the last step holds as long as
p\gg\mu^{2}r^{2}d^{-2}\log^{2}d

and
\sigma/\lambda_{\min}^{\star}\ll\sqrt{p/(rd\log^{2}d)}
.
In addition, from (
211
), we observe that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}&\leq\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}\\
&\lesssim o\left(1\right)\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}\leq o\left(1\right)\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}+\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\},\end{split}

from which we can deduce that

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\}.

As a consequence, one immediately obtains

\displaystyle\big\|\widehat{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}\Bigg\}\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\}.

(215)

–

Combining (
208
), (
210
)
and (
215
) and the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}

\displaystyle-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{r\log d}\,\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\}.

(216)

Comparing this bound with the first claim of the lemma, we see that
the claim can be established as long as we can show that

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\mu/d}.

(217)

To justify this bound (
217
), we make use of Lemma
5.14

to derive that

\displaystyle\left|\big(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big)_{m}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\big(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{m}\right|+\left|\big(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big)_{m}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}+\left|\big(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big)_{m}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}})\sqrt{r\log d}\,\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\}

for each
m\in[d]
. Maximizing over
m\in[d]
gives that

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}\right)\sqrt{r\log d}\,\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\}

(218)

\displaystyle\ll\max\big\{\sqrt{\mu/d},\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}\big\},

(219)

where we use the condition that
\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}\right)\sqrt{r\log d}\ll 1
. Apply the triangle inequality to yield

\displaystyle\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}+\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}\leq o\left(1\right)\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{\infty}+\sqrt{\mu/d}.

These allow us to establish the claim (
217
), which
in turn finishes the proof for the first claim of this lemma.

•

The second claim (
83
) of this lemma follows immediately from (
217
)
and (
218
).

•

It remains to prove the last claim (
84
). Recall the definition of
\lambda_{\tau}
and
\lambda_{\tau}^{\left(m\right)}
in (
71
). We can decompose

\displaystyle\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}^{\left(m\right)},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle-\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}^{\left(m\right)}-p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle}_{=:\,\beta_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)})^{\otimes 3}-(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle}_{=:\,\beta_{2}}

(220)

In what follows, we will control
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
seperately.

For
\beta_{1}
, we note that all non-zero entries of
\bm{T}^{\left(m\right)}-\bm{T}
are located in the
m
th slices, and are independent of
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
. This type of quantities have appeared many times and we omit the detailed proof for conciseness. By the Bernstein inequality, one can show that with probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
,

\displaystyle\Big|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}^{\left(m\right)}-p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle\Big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d^{2}p}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}.

(221)

Next, we turn to
\beta_{2}
. From our sample size and noise condition, Lemma
D.1
and Corollary
D.3
demonstrates that with probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
,

\displaystyle\left\|p^{-1}\bm{T}\right\|\leq\left\|p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|+\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|\leq\left\|p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|+\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{\star}.

where we use the fact that the tensor spectral norm is always less
than or equal to that of its matricization. By the definition of the operator norm, one has

\displaystyle\left|\beta_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3\,\big|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\big(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big)\big\rangle\big|+3\,\big|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)})\otimes\big(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big)^{\otimes 2}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\quad+\big|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},\big(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big)^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\|p^{-1}\bm{T}\right\|\Big(\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{2}+\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{2}^{2}+\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}\big\|_{2}^{3}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}

(222)

where we use (
216
) and (
217
) in the last step and the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\mu r\log d/d}\ll 1
.

Combining (
221
) and (
222
) immediately establishes (
84
).

B.7
Proof of Lemma
B.3

Recalling the definitions of
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
and
\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
in (
183
) and (
70c
), respectively, we observe that
\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

is independent of
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
conditional
on
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{-m}}\left(\bm{E}\right)
and
\bm{g}
.

•

The
m
-th entry of
\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

can be written as

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{m,:}\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}=\big(p^{-1}\big(\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{(m)}\big)\times_{3}\bm{\theta}^{\tau,(m)}\big)_{m,:}\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad=\underbrace{\sum_{j,k\in[d]}T_{m,j,k}^{\star}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)}_{=:\alpha_{1}}+\underbrace{\sum_{j,k\in[d]}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}p^{-1}E_{m,j,k}\chi_{m,j,k}}_{=:\alpha_{2}}.

(223)

–

For the first term
\alpha_{1}
, it is easily seen from (
200
) and incoherence conditions
that

\displaystyle L_{1}:=\max_{j,k\in[d]}\left|T_{m,j,k}^{\star}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)\right|

\displaystyle\leq p^{-1}\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\sqrt{\log d}}{d^{2}p}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty};

and

\displaystyle V_{1}:=\sum_{j,k\in[d]}T_{m,j,k}^{\star 2}\big(\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)^{2}\big(\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\leq p^{-1}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log d}{d^{2}p}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}.

Apply the Bernstein inequality to yield that with probability at least

1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j,k\in[d]}T_{m,j,k}^{\star}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}(p^{-1}\chi_{m,j,k}-1)\lesssim\sqrt{V_{1}\log d}+L_{1}\log d\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty},

where the last inequality holds as long as

p\gg d^{-2}\log^{2}d
.

–

Regarding
\alpha_{2}
(cf. (
223
)),
it is straightforward to compute that

\displaystyle L_{2}

\displaystyle:=\big\|p^{-1}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}E_{m,j,k}\chi_{m,j,k}\big\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq\frac{\sigma}{p}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\,\lesssim\frac{\sigma}{p}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{d}}\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty},

with
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
denoting the sub-exponential norm, and

\displaystyle V_{2}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big(\sum\nolimits_{j,k\in[d]}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}p^{-1}E_{m,j,k}\chi_{m,j,k}\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\,=\sum_{j,k\in[d]}\big(\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)^{2}\big(\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[p^{-2}E_{m,j,k}^{2}\chi_{m,j,k}\big]

\displaystyle\,\leq\frac{\sigma^{2}}{p}\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}^{2}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}rd\log d}{p}\,\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}.

Then the Bernstein inequality reveals that with probability at least

1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)
,

\displaystyle\Big|p^{-1}\sum_{j,k\in[d]}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}E_{m,j,k}\chi_{m,j,k}\Big|\lesssim L_{2}\log^{2}d+\sqrt{V_{2}\log d}\leq\sigma\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty},

where the last inequality follows from our sample size condition.

–

Substituting these into (
223
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\Big|\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{m,:}\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\Big|\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}\Bigg\}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}.

(224)

•

For the remaining entries of
\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
,
we have

\displaystyle\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{i,:}\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad=\theta_{m}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{j:j\neq m}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big(T_{i,j,m}^{\star}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,m}-1)+p^{-1}E_{i,j,m}\chi_{i,j,m}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\overline{v}_{m}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\Big(\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big(T_{i,m,k}^{\star}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,m,k}-1)+p^{-1}E_{i,m,k}\chi_{i,m,k}\big)\Big)

for any
i\neq m
. From Lemma
D.9

and (
200
), we have, with probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)
,
that

\displaystyle\big(\theta_{m}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{i:i\neq m}\Big(\sum_{j:j\neq m}T_{i,j,m}^{\star}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,m}-1)\Big)^{2}+\big(\overline{v}_{m}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)^{2}\sum_{i:i\neq m}\Big(\sum_{k\in[d]}T_{i,m,k}^{\star}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,m,k}-1)\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log d}{d^{2}p}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}.

Combined with (
198
) and (
200
), Lemma
D.10
reveals that with
probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)
,

\displaystyle\big(\theta_{m}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\sum_{i:i\neq m}\Big(p^{-1}\sum_{j:j\neq m}\overline{u}_{j}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}E_{i,j,m}\chi_{i,j,m}\Big)^{2}+\big(\overline{v}_{m}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)^{2}\sum_{i:i\neq m}\Big(p^{-1}\sum_{k\in[d]}\theta_{k}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}E_{i,m,k}\chi_{i,m,k}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{p}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}^{2}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{\sigma^{2}d}{p}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}^{2}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\log^{5}d}{p^{2}}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}\big\|\bm{\theta}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sigma^{2}rd\log d}{p}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2},

which implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{i:i\neq m}\Big(\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)_{i,:}\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left\{\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log d}{d^{2}p}+\frac{\sigma^{2}rd\log d}{p}\right\}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}^{2}.

(225)

•

Therefore, combine (
224
) and (
225
)
to obtain that

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\widehat{\bm{M}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big)\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\Bigg\{\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\log d}{d\sqrt{p}}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{rd\log^{2}d}{p}}\Bigg\}\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{\infty}.

B.8
Proof of Lemma
5.16

By definition, the only possible difference between
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
and

\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
lies in how their global signs are chosen. To show that
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}=\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
,
we first claim for the moment that

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle-\lambda_{1}^{\star}\right|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\cdot\lambda_{1}^{\star},

(226)

where
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}
is defined in (
79
). Given that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\ll 1
under our sample size, noise and rank condition, this immediately implies that
\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle>0
. Consequently, by construction, the global signs of
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}
and

\bm{\nu}^{\tau}
coincide. Moreover, from (
84
) and the condition that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{loo}}\sqrt{\mu r\log d/d}\ll 1
, one also knows that
\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}^{\left(m\right)},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle>0
and hence the global signs of
\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
and

\bm{\nu}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
also coincide.

In addition, recall that
\lambda_{\tau}=\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},({\bm{\nu}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle
. One thus has

\displaystyle\lambda_{\tau}=\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},({\bm{\nu}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle=\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle,

(227)

which taken collectively with (
226
) justifies (
85
).

The rest of the proof then comes down to establishing the claim (
226
). Towards this, we first decompose

\displaystyle\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle-\lambda_{1}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle-\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle}_{=:\,\beta_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle-\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle}_{=:\,\beta_{2}}+\underbrace{\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle-\lambda_{1}^{\star}}_{=:\,\beta_{3}}.

(228)

In what follows, we shall upper bound these three terms separately.

B.8.1
Controlling
\beta_{1}

Let us start with
\beta_{1}
, For simplicity of notation, let us
define
\bm{\Delta}_{1}:=\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}
. By construction,
\bm{T}
and
\bm{T}^{\star}
are symmetric.
We then can expand

\displaystyle\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}+\bm{\Delta}_{1})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle+3\,\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}\otimes(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 2}\big\rangle+3\,\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle.

(229)

We first look at the first term of (
229
) which only consists of
\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}
.
As shown in (
173
), with probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-11}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\left(p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right)\times_{3}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|\lesssim\frac{\mu\sqrt{r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\sqrt{\log d}}{d\sqrt{p}}+\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle\right|\leq\left\|\left(p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right)\times_{3}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\lambda_{\min}^{\star},

where we recall the definition of
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}
in (
79
).
As for the term linear in
\bm{\Delta}_{1}
, by Lemma
5.13
, we know that
\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}
. As a result, one
has

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}\otimes(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 2}\big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\left(p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right)\times_{3}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|_{2}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}^{2}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}.

We then turn to the quadratic terms in
\bm{\Delta}_{1}
. Similar to the above arguments, one can deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle\right|\leq\left\|\left(p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right)\times_{3}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}^{3}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}.

Finally, we can simply upper bound the last term in (
173
) by

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle\right|\leq\left\|p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{3}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}^{3}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}\ll\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}^{3}\lambda_{\min}^{\star},

where the last step is due to the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{op}}\ll 1
. By our sample size, noise and rank conditions, one has
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\ll 1
. Putting these bounds together reveals that

\left|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle\right|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}.

(230)

B.8.2
Controlling
\beta_{2}

Recall the definition of
\beta_{2}
in (
228
) and
\bm{\Delta}_{1}=\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}
. We can further decompose

\displaystyle\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}-(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=3\,\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{1}\big\rangle+3\,\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle+\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle.

We first consider the first term which is linear in
\bm{\Delta}_{1}
. Since
\bm{T}^{\star}
is a symmetric tensor, we have

\displaystyle\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{1}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}=\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{2}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}=\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{3}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}=\sum\nolimits_{s\in\left[r\right]}\lambda_{1}^{\star}\left\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\rangle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star}\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star\top}.

By Lemma
D.1
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{1}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|\leq\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left|\lambda_{1}^{\star}\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle\right|\big\|\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big\|^{2}\leq(\lambda_{1}^{\star}+\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\sqrt{\mu/d})\lesssim\lambda_{1}^{\star},

which arises from
\max_{s\neq i}\left|\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle\right|\leq\sqrt{\mu/d}

and
\big\|\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big\|\lesssim 1
as long as
r\sqrt{\mu/d}\ll 1
.
As a result, one has

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{1}\big\rangle\right|\leq\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{1}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|_{2}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\lambda_{1}^{\star}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\lambda_{1}^{\star}.

In a similar manner, we also know that

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle\right|\leq\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{1}\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}^{2}\lambda_{1}^{\star}.

Finally, using the fact that the tensor spectral norm is always less
than or equal to that of its matricization, we find that

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle\right|\leq\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{3}\leq\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{3}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}^{3}\lambda_{1}^{\star}.

Combining this with the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\ll 1
, we conclude that

\left|\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}-(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle\right|\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\lambda_{1}^{\star}.

(231)

B.8.3
Controlling
\beta_{3}

It remains to control
\beta_{3}
. Straightforward calculation reveals that

\displaystyle\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\in\left[r\right]}\lambda_{s}^{\star}\big\langle(\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star})^{\otimes 3},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle=\lambda_{1}^{\star}\left\|\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{6}+\sum_{s:s\neq 1}\lambda_{s}^{\star}\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle^{6}.

By the incoherence conditions, we can upper bound

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle\bm{T}^{\star},(\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle-\lambda_{1}^{\star}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{s:s\neq 1}\lambda_{1}^{\star}\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle^{6}\leq r\,\max_{s:s\neq 1}\left|\big\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{s}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star}\big\rangle\right|^{6}\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\leq\frac{\mu^{3}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3}}.

(232)

B.8.4
Combining
\beta_{1}
,
\beta_{2}
and
\beta_{3}

Putting (
230
), (
231
) and (
232
)
together, we find that

\displaystyle\left|\big\langle p^{-1}\bm{T},(\overline{\bm{u}}^{\tau})^{\otimes 3}\big\rangle-\lambda_{1}^{\star}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\lambda_{1}^{\star}+\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3}}\leq\left(\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}+\frac{\mu^{3}r}{d^{3}}\right)\lambda_{1}^{\star}\asymp\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\lambda_{1}^{\star},

where the last step follows from the condition
\mu\leq d
,
r\ll\sqrt{d/\mu}
and the definition
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\geq\sqrt{\mu r/d}
(cf. (
79
)).

B.9
Proof of Lemma
5.17

We first show that for each
i\in\left[r\right]
,
\big(\bm{w}_{i},\lambda_{i}\big)
and
\big(\bm{w}_{i}^{\left(m\right)},\lambda_{i}^{\left(m\right)}\big)
(returned by
\textsc{Prune}(
) in Algorithm
3
and Algorithm
6
respectively) satisfy (
80
), (
82
) and (
84
); in other words, we want to show that they correspond to the same index
\tau\in\left[L\right]
(and are hence produced using the same Gaussian random vector
\bm{g}^{\tau}
).
The proof idea is this: given that the proposed algorithms select the pair with the largest spectral gap in each round of
\textsc{Prune}(
), it suffices to ensure that
there is sufficient separation between the largest and the second largest spectral gaps (so that both algorithms can identify the same
\tau
).

By Lemma
5.12
and union bounds, we know that with probability at least
1-\delta
, for each
i\in\left[r\right]
,

\displaystyle\Delta^{\left(1\right)}_{i}-\Delta^{\left(2\right)}_{i}\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/\big(r\sqrt{\log d}\big),

(233)

where we recall that
\Delta_{i}^{\tau}:=\gamma_{i}^{\star\tau}-\max_{j:j\neq i}\big|\gamma_{j}^{\star\tau}\big|
, and
\Delta_{i}^{\left(1\right)}\geq\Delta_{i}^{\left(2\right)}\geq\dots\geq\Delta_{i}^{\left(L\right)}
denote the order statistics of
\big\{\Delta_{i}^{\tau}\big\}_{\tau=1}^{L}
in descending order. As shown in the proof of Lemma
5.13
, the spectral gap of
\bm{M}^{\tau}
is well approximated by
\max_{i}\Delta_{i}^{\tau}
, namely,

\displaystyle\Big|\max_{1\leq i\leq r}\Delta_{i}^{\tau}-\big(\sigma_{1}\left(\bm{M}^{\tau}\right)-\sigma_{2}\left(\bm{M}^{\tau}\right)\big)\Big|\lesssim\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\bm{M}^{\tau\star}\big\|\ll\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}{r\sqrt{\log d}}

under our sample size, noise and rank conditions (
67
). Moreover, from Lemma
B.2
, we see that
\bm{M}^{\tau}
and
\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}
are extremely close in terms of the spectral norm, i.e.

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{M}^{\tau}-\bm{M}^{\tau,\left(m\right)}\big\|\ll\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}{r\sqrt{\log d}}.

This implies that the perturbation incurred by the leave-out-one procedure is relatively small compared to the difference between the largest and the second largest spectral gaps of
\bm{M}^{\tau}
. Consequently, the leave-one-out estimates
\big\{\big(\bm{w}_{i}^{\left(m\right)},\lambda_{i}^{\left(m\right)}\big)\big\}_{i=1}^{r}
returned by Algorithm
6
and the true estimates
\big\{\big(\bm{w}_{i},\lambda_{i}\big)\big\}_{i=1}^{r}
should correspond to the same trials and should be generated by the same set of Gaussian random vectors. As a result, they obey (
69a
), (
69b
) and (
69c
) for all
1\leq m\leq d
.

From the discussion above, we also know that as long as
\sigma_{1}\left(\bm{M}^{\tau}\right)-\sigma_{2}\left(\bm{M}^{\tau}\right)\gtrsim\lambda_{\min}^{\star}
, one has
\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}
for some
i\in\left[r\right]
. This is an immediate consequence of Lemma
5.13
and the fact that the spectral gap of
\bm{M}^{\tau}
and
\max_{i}\Delta_{i}^{\tau}
are extremely close.

It remains to show that our pruning procedure can return estimates of tensor factors without duplicates.
Suppose that there exist
1\leq\tau_{1}\neq\tau_{2}\leq L
such that
\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}
and
\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{2}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}
for some
i\in\left[r\right]
. By the triangle inequality, one has

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}},\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{2}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\|\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\langle\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\rangle+\left\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{2}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{2}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\geq 1-\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{2}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}-\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{2}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq 1-2\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}-\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}^{2}\geq 1-3\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}},

provided that
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\ll 1
. In addition, for any
j\neq i,j\in\left[r\right]
, we know that exists some
1\leq\tau_{3}\leq L
such that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{3}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{j}^{\star}\right\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}.

Recall our incoherence condition in (
8c
). It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}},\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{3}}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\left\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{j}^{\star}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\rangle+\left\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{3}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\rangle+\left\langle\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{3}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{j}^{\star}\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\overline{\bm{u}}_{j}^{\star}\right\rangle\right|+\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{3}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{j}^{\star}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}\left\|\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{3}}-\overline{\bm{u}}_{j}^{\star}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mu/d}+2\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}+\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}^{2}\leq\sqrt{\mu/d}+3\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\ll 1-3\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}},

with the proviso that
\mu\ll d
and
\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{proj}}\ll 1
.

The above argument reveals a clear separation between
\left|\left\langle\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}},\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{3}}\right\rangle\right|
and
\left|\left\langle\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{1}},\bm{\nu}^{\tau_{2}}\right\rangle\right|
. As an immediate consequence, the proposed pruning procedure successfully removes all duplication while securing an estimate for each tensor factor.

B.10
Proof of Corollary
5.11

Fix any arbitrary small constant
\delta>0
. From Theorems
5.9
-
5.10
and the assumptions of Theorem
2.8
, one knows that with probability exceeding
1-\delta
, there exists a permutation
\pi(\cdot):[d]\mapsto[d]
such that for all
1\leq i\leq r
,

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{w}^{i}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{\pi(i)}\big\|_{2}\leq\delta,\qquad\big\|\bm{w}^{i}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{\pi(i)}\big\|_{\infty}\leq\delta\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}},\qquad\big|\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{\pi(i)}^{\star}\big|\leq\delta\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{w}^{i}-\bm{w}^{i,\left(m\right)}\big\|_{2}\leq\delta\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}},\qquad\big|\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\left(m\right)}\big|\leq\delta\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star},\qquad\big|\big(\bm{w}^{i}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{\pi(i)}\big)_{m}\big\|_{2}\leq\delta\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}}

for some
0<\delta\ll 1/(\mu^{3/2}r)<1
. To prove the corollary, we shall just combine the above results.

Without loss of generality, assume that
\pi(i)=i
for each
i\in\left[r\right]
. Given that
\delta\ll 1
and
\kappa\asymp 1
, by the triangle inequality, one has
\lambda_{i}\asymp\lambda_{i}^{\star}
for all
i\in\left[r\right]
, which further implies that

\displaystyle\big|\lambda^{1/3}_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 1/3}\big|\lesssim\frac{\big|\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star}\big|}{\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}}\lesssim\delta\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}.

Consequently, we can apply the triangle inequality to demonstrate that: for each
1\leq i\leq r
,

\displaystyle\big\|\lambda^{1/3}_{i}\bm{w}^{i}-\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\big\|_{2}\leq\big|\lambda^{1/3}_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 1/3}\big|\big\|\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\big\|_{2}+\lambda^{\star 1/3}_{i}\big\|\bm{w}^{i}-\overline{\bm{u}}^{\star}_{i}\big\|_{2}\lesssim\delta\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}.

Arguing similarly, we also see that

\displaystyle\big\|\lambda^{1/3}_{i}\bm{w}^{i}-\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

\displaystyle\big\|\lambda^{1/3}_{i}\bm{w}^{i}-\big(\lambda_{i}^{(m)}\big)^{1/3}\bm{w}^{i,(m)}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3},

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(\big(\lambda_{i}^{(m)}\big)^{1/3}\bm{w}^{i,(m)}-\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\Big)_{m}\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}

hold for all
i\in\left[r\right]
and
m\in\left[d\right]
.
Recall that
\bm{U}^{0}=\big[\lambda_{i}^{1/3}\bm{w}^{i}\big]_{1\leq i\leq r}
. One can deduce that

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{0}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\lesssim\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{0}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{r}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\lesssim\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{U}^{0}-\bm{U}^{0,(m)}\big\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{r}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\lesssim\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

\displaystyle\big\|\big(\bm{U}^{0,(m)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\big)_{m,:}\big\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\frac{r}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\lesssim\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

where we have used the condition that
\kappa\asymp 1
and the fact that
\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\sqrt{r}\,\lambda_{\min}^{\star 1/3}
and
\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\geq\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}/\sqrt{d}
.

Appendix C
Proof of Corollary
2.9

This section establishes Corollary
2.9
. First of all, it is easy to see that: given the estimation accuracy established in Theorem
2.8
, the permutation matrices that best match
\bm{U}^{t}
to
\bm{U}^{\star}
remain unchanged as
t
increases. Therefore, we assume without loss of generality that
\bm{I}_{r}=\arg\min_{\bm{\Pi}\in\mathsf{perm}_{r}}\left\|\bm{U}^{t}\bm{\Pi}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|
for all
t\geq 0
.

Suppose that
r\sqrt{\mu/d}\ll 1
. We claim for the moment that: if a matrix
\bm{U}=[\bm{u}_{1},\cdots,\bm{u}_{r}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

for any
0\leq\delta\ll 1/(\mu^{3/2}r)\leq 1
, then one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\delta\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\left\|\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\delta\sqrt{\mu^{3}r}\,\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty},

(234)

where
\bm{T}:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{u}_{i}
. As already shown in the analysis of Theorem
2.8
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(C_{1}\frac{\rho^{t+1}}{\mu^{3/2}r}+C_{2}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{U}^{t}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(C_{3}\rho^{t+1}\frac{\rho^{t+1}}{\mu^{3/2}r}+C_{4}\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}^{\star}}\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}\right)\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty},

from which Corollary
2.9
follows immediately.

It remains to prove the claim (
234
). For notational convenience, let us define
\bm{\Delta}:=\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star}
and
\bm{\Delta}_{s}:=\bm{u}_{s}-\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}

for each
1\leq s\leq r
. Then we can expand

\displaystyle\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\otimes 3}-\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 3}=\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}+\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}+\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}+\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}+\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 3}.

(235)

(1) Euclidean loss.

We first look at the loss measured by
\|\cdot\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. In view of the symmetric structure of tensors, it suffices to control
\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}
,
\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}
and
\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 3}
.

Let us define
\bm{W}_{1}:=\left[\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\right]_{1\leq s\leq r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times r}
and
\bm{W}_{2}:=\left[\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\right]_{1\leq s\leq r}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times r}
. Recalling the fact that
\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|\leq\big\|\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star}\big\|\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}

(established in Lemma
D.1
), we have

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{W}_{1}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{W}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\left\|\bm{W}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{W}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{W}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\left\|\bm{W}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 3}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{\Delta}\bm{W}_{2}^{\top}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|\left\|\bm{W}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\left\|\bm{W}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

It then suffices to control
\left\|\bm{W}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
and
\left\|\bm{W}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
. If
\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\sqrt{r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}
, then it is easy to bound

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{W}_{1}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{s}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\delta^{2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3},

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{W}_{2}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\right\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{s}\right\|_{2}^{4}\leq\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{\Delta}_{s}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\leq\left\|\bm{\Delta}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{4}\leq\delta^{4}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}.

Therefore, one has

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\sqrt{r}\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

(236)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star},

(237)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 3}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta^{3}r^{3/2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star}.

(238)

Since
0\leq\delta\ll r^{-1}\leq 1
, combining (
236
), (
237
) and (
238
) with the fact that
\left\|\bm{T^{\star}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\geq\sqrt{r}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}/2
(established in Lemma
D.1
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|\lesssim\delta\sqrt{r}\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\lesssim\delta\left\|\bm{T^{\star}}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

(239)

(2)
\ell_{\infty}
loss.

Next, we turn to the
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\infty}
loss. Again, it suffices to focus on
\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}
,
\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}
and
\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 3}
.
From (
104
), (
105
) and (
106
) shown in the proof of Lemma
5.1
, one has

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\left(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right)^{\otimes 2}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}\Big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\leq\delta\frac{\mu^{3/2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}},

(240)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 2}\Big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{2}\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}\mu^{3/2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}},

(241)

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\bm{\Delta}_{s}^{\otimes 3}\Big\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\delta^{3}\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{3}\leq\frac{\delta^{3}\mu^{3/2}r^{3/2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}}.

(242)

Putting (
240
), (
241
)
and (
242
) together with the condition that
0<\delta\ll r^{-1}\leq 1
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\delta\mu^{3/2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}}.

In addition, from the lower bound on
\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\geq\frac{1}{d^{3/2}}\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\gtrsim\sqrt{\frac{r}{d^{3}}}\,\lambda_{\min}^{\star},

which allows us to conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\mu^{3}r}\,\delta\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}.

Appendix D
Auxiliary lemmas

This section gathers several auxiliary lemmas that prove useful when establishing our main results.

D.1
Statements of auxiliary lemmas

We begin by stating all auxiliary lemmas formally, with the proofs postponed to subsequent subsections. We shall define

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star}:=[\overline{\bm{u}}_{1}^{\star},\cdots,\overline{\bm{u}}_{r}^{\star}],\qquad\text{with}\qquad\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}:=\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}/\left\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}.

(243)

Lemma D.1
.

Suppose that Assumption
2.1
holds, and assume that
r\sqrt{\mu/d}\leq c_{1}
for some sufficiently small universal constant
c_{2}>0
. Then for
d
sufficiently large, the matrices
\bm{A}^{\star}
,
\bm{B}^{\star}
and
\bm{U}_{\mathsf{orth}}^{\star}
(defined respectively in (
24
), (
63
) and (
54
)) obey

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}^{\star}\leq\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}^{\star},\quad\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2\mu r}\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}},\quad\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\mu r}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star},\quad\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\frac{\mu\sqrt{2r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d},

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|=\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\left(1+O\left(r\sqrt{\tfrac{\mu}{d}}\right)\right),\qquad\lambda_{i}(\bm{B}^{\star})=\lambda_{(i)}^{\star 2}\left(1+O\left(r\sqrt{\tfrac{\mu}{d}}\right)\right),\penalty\ \penalty\ i\in\left[r\right],

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{B}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq 2\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2},\qquad\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}_{\mathsf{orth}}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\mu r}{d}},\qquad\|\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star\top}\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star}-\bm{I}\|\leq r\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d}}.

Here,
\|\bm{A}\|_{2,\infty}:=\max_{i}\|\bm{A}_{i,:}\|_{2}
,
\lambda_{(i)}^{\star}
stands for the
i
-th largest value in
\{\lambda_{i}^{\star}\}_{1\leq i\leq r}
(or equivalently
\{\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{3}\}_{1\leq i\leq r}
), and
\lambda_{i}(\bm{B}^{\star})
represents the
i
-th largest eigenvalue of
\bm{B}^{\star}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
D.2
.
∎

Lemma D.2
.

Let
\bm{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}
be a random order-3 tensor with independent entries
\left\{R_{i,j,k}\right\}_{i,j,k\in\left[d\right]}
obeying

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i,j,k}\right]=0,\quad\max_{i,j,k\in\left[d\right]}\left|R_{i,j,k}\right|\leq B.

Define

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}^{2}:=\max_{j,k\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[R^{2}_{i,j,k}]+\max_{i,k\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[R^{2}_{i,j,k}]+\max_{i,j\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[R^{2}_{i,j,k}].

(244)

Then with probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{R}\right\|\lesssim B\log^{3}d+\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}\log^{5/2}d.

(245)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.3
.
∎

An immediate consequence of this lemma is the following:

Corollary D.3
.

With probability at least
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}(\bm{T}^{\star})-\bm{T}^{\star}\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\mu r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\log^{3}d}{d^{3/2}p}+\frac{\mu\sqrt{r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\log^{5/2}d}{d\sqrt{p}};

(246)

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}(\bm{E})\big\|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\big(\log^{7/2}d+\sqrt{dp}\,\log^{5/2}d\big).

(247)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.3
.
∎

Lemma D.4
.

Suppose that
p\gtrsim d^{-2}\log^{3}d
and that
\mu\log^{2}d\lesssim d
.
Then for any fixed vector
\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, with probability

1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\left\|\left(p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right)\times_{3}\bm{w}\right\|\lesssim\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{dp}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\frac{\sigma\log^{5/2}d}{p}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}},

where
\times_{3}
is defined in Section
2.4
. The results also holds if we replace
\times_{3}
with
\times_{1}
or
\times_{2}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
D.4
. ∎

Lemma D.5
.

Let
\{X_{i,j}\}_{1\leq i\leq r,1\leq j\leq L}
be a sequence of i.i.d. standard Gaussian random variables, where
r\geq 2
and
L\geq 1
. Consider some quantities
\kappa\geq 1,\Delta>0,0<\delta<1/2
. There exists some universal constant
C>0
such that if

\displaystyle L\geq Cr^{2\kappa^{2}}\big(\kappa\sqrt{\log r}+\Delta\big)\exp(\Delta^{2})\log\frac{1}{\delta},

then with probability at least
1-\delta
, there exists some
1\leq j_{0}\leq L
such that

\displaystyle X_{1,j_{0}}>\kappa\max_{i:1<i\leq r}\left|X_{i,j_{0}}\right|+\Delta.

In addition, define
\Delta_{j}:=X_{1,j}-\kappa\max_{i:1<i\leq r}\left|X_{i,j}\right|
for each
1\leq j\leq L
. Then with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{(1)}-\Delta_{(2)}\gtrsim\frac{\delta}{\sqrt{\log L}+\sqrt{\log\left(1/\delta\right)}}.

where
\Delta_{(1)}\geq\Delta_{(2)}\geq\dots\geq\Delta_{(L)}
denote the order statistics of
\left\{\Delta_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{L}
in descending order.

Proof.

See Appendix
D.5
.
∎

Lemma D.6
.

Let
\bm{U}
(resp.
\bm{V}
) be a
d\times r
matrix with orthonormal columns. Suppose that
\|\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}-\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top}\|\leq\delta
. Then for any unit vector
\bm{u}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
lying in
\mathsf{span}(\bm{U})
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}}(\bm{u}_{0})\big\|_{2}\geq\sqrt{1-\delta^{2}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}^{\perp}}(\bm{u}_{0})\big\|_{2}\leq\delta,

(248)

where we denote by
\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}}(\bm{u}_{0}):=\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top}\bm{u}_{0}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}^{\perp}}(\bm{u}_{0})=\big(\bm{I}_{d}-\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top}\big)\bm{u}_{0}
.

Proof.

See Appendix
D.6
.
∎

Additionally, we record several facts concerning the set of Bernoulli random variables
\{\chi_{i,j,k}\}_{1\leq i,j,k\leq d}
. We recall that

\displaystyle\chi_{i,j,k}:=\ind\{(i,j,k)\in\Omega\},

(249)

which is a Bernoulli random variable with mean
p
.

Lemma D.7
.

Suppose that
p\gtrsim d^{-2}\log d
. With probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}T^{\star 2}_{i,j,k}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp},\qquad 1\leq k\leq d.

(250)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.7
.
∎

Lemma D.8
.

Suppose that
p\gtrsim d^{-2}\log^{2}d
. With probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\left(p^{-1}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\right)^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}d^{2}/p,\qquad 1\leq k\leq d.

(251)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.8
.
∎

Lemma D.9
.

Suppose
p\gtrsim d^{-2}\log d
and
\mu\log^{2}d\lesssim d
. Consider any fixed vector
\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and any
1\leq i\leq d
. With probability exceeding
1-O(d^{-10})
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\Big(\sum_{k\in[d]}T^{\star}_{i,j,k}w_{k}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)\Big)^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}.

(252)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.9
.
∎

Lemma D.10
.

Consider any fixed vector
\bm{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. With probability
1-O(d^{-10})
one has

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\Big(\sum_{k\in[d]}w_{k}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\Big)^{2}\lesssim\sigma^{2}dp\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\log^{5}d,\qquad 1\leq i\leq d.

(253)

Proof.

See Appendix
D.10
.
∎

D.2
Proof of Lemma
D.1

1.

To begin with, the incoherence condition (
8b
) gives

\displaystyle\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\langle\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star\otimes 3},\sum\nolimits_{i\in[r]}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star\otimes 3}\Big\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star\otimes 3}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq r}\left\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star\otimes 3},\bm{u}_{j}^{\star\otimes 3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{6}+\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq r}\left\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{u}_{j}^{\star}\right\rangle^{3}

\displaystyle\leq r\max_{1\leq i\leq r}\left\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{6}+r^{2}\max_{1\leq i\neq j\leq r}\big|\left\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{u}_{j}^{\star}\right\rangle^{3}\big|

\displaystyle\leq r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}+r^{2}\left(\frac{\mu}{d}\right)^{3/2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}2r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2},

where we use the fact that
\left\langle\bm{u}^{\otimes 3},\bm{v}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle=\left\langle\bm{u},\bm{v}\right\rangle^{3}
in (i), and (ii) arises due to the condition that
3r\leq\sqrt{d/\mu}\leq(d/\mu)^{3/2}
. Using a similar argument, we also know that
\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\geq r\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2}/2
. This combined with the incoherence condition in (
8a
) yields

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}=\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d^{3}}}\left\|\bm{T}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2\mu r}}{d^{3/2}}\lambda_{\max}^{\star}.

2.

For any
1\leq i\leq d
, the
\ell_{2}
norm of the
i
-th row of
\bm{A}^{\star}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\big\|\bm{A}^{\star}_{i,:}\big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\big(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\big)_{i}\big(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\big)^{\top}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\big(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\big)_{i}^{2}\left\|\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{1\leq s_{1}\neq s_{2}\leq r}\big(\bm{u}_{s_{1}}^{\star}\big)_{i}\big(\bm{u}_{s_{2}}^{\star}\big)_{i}\left\langle\bm{u}_{s_{1}}^{\star}\otimes\bm{u}_{s_{1}}^{\star},\bm{u}_{s_{2}}^{\star}\otimes\bm{u}_{s_{2}}^{\star}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq r\,\max_{s\in[r]}\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\|_{\infty}^{2}\max_{s\in[r]}\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\|_{2}^{4}+r^{2}\,\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\|_{\infty}^{2}\max_{s_{1}\neq s_{2}}\left\langle\bm{u}_{s_{1}}^{\star},\bm{u}_{s_{2}}^{\star}\right\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\left(\frac{\mu r}{d}+\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}}{d^{2}}\right)\leq\frac{2\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{d},

(254)

where the first inequality follows from the fact that
\left\langle\bm{u}\otimes\bm{u},\bm{v}\otimes\bm{v}\right\rangle=\left\langle\bm{u},\bm{v}\right\rangle^{2}
, the second inequality holds true due to (
8b
) and (
8c
), and the last inequality holds as long as
r\leq d/\mu
. This immediately yields the advertised bound on

\big\|\bm{A}^{\star}\big\|_{2,\infty}
.

3.

For any
\underline{j}\in\left[d\right]^{2}
(which corresponds to
(j_{1},j_{2})
), the
\ell_{2}
norm of the
\underline{j}
-th column of
\bm{A}^{\star}
can be upper bounded similarly by

\displaystyle\Big\|\bm{A}^{\star}_{:,\underline{j}}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\Big\|\sum\nolimits_{1\leq s\leq r}\big(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\otimes\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\big)_{\underline{j}}\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\Big\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq s\leq r}\big(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\big)_{j_{1}}^{2}\big(\bm{u}_{s}^{\star}\big)_{j_{2}}^{2}\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{s_{1}\neq s_{2}}\big(\bm{u}_{s_{1}}^{\star}\big)_{j_{1}}\big(\bm{u}_{s_{1}}^{\star}\big)_{j_{2}}\big(\bm{u}_{s_{2}}^{\star}\big)_{j_{1}}\big(\bm{u}_{s_{2}}^{\star}\big)_{j_{2}}\left\langle\bm{u}_{s_{1}}^{\star},\bm{u}_{s_{2}}^{\star}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq r\,\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\|_{\infty}^{4}\max_{s\in[r]}\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\|_{2}^{2}+r^{2}\,\max_{1\leq s\leq r}\|\bm{u}^{\star}_{s}\|_{\infty}^{4}\max_{1\leq s_{1}\neq s_{2}\leq r}\big|\left\langle\bm{u}_{s_{1}}^{\star},\bm{u}_{s_{2}}^{\star}\right\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\leq\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}r}{d^{2}}+\frac{\mu^{5/2}r^{2}}{d^{5/2}}\right)\leq\frac{2\mu^{2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{d^{2}},

(255)

where the second inequality is valid due to (
8b
) and (
8c
), and the last inequality holds as long as
r\leq\sqrt{d/\mu}
. This yields the claimed bound regarding
\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\|_{2,\infty}
.

4.

Regarding the spectrum of
\bm{A}^{\star}
,
\bm{B}^{\star}
,
\bm{U}_{\mathsf{orth}}^{\star}
and
\overline{\bm{U}}^{\star}
, we refer the reader to the proof of
[
CLC+20
, Corollary 1]
.

5.

We now move on to
\left\|\bm{B}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}
. For any
i\in\left[d\right]
, it is seen that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{B}^{\star}_{i,:}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in[d]}\left(\bm{A}_{i,:}^{\star}\bm{A}_{j,:}^{\star\top}\right)^{2}=\bm{A}_{i,:}^{\star}\Big(\sum_{j\in[d]}\bm{A}_{j,:}^{*\top}\bm{A}_{j,:}^{\star}\Big)\bm{A}_{i,:}^{*\top}=\bm{A}_{i,:}^{\star}\left(\bm{A}^{\star\top}\bm{A}^{\star}\right)\bm{A}_{i,:}^{*\top}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|\left\|\bm{A}_{i,:}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\left\|\bm{A}_{i,:}^{\star}\right\|_{2}^{2}.

Here, the last line makes use of the bound
\|\bm{A}^{\star}\|\leq\lambda_{\max}^{\star}(1+O(r\sqrt{\mu/d}))\leq 2\lambda_{\max}^{\star}
, which holds if
r\sqrt{\mu/d}\leq c_{1}
for some sufficiently small constant
c_{1}>0
.
It then follows from (
254
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{B}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\sqrt{2}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq 2\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d}}.

(256)

D.3
Proof of Lemma
D.2
and Corollary
D.3

D.3.1
Proof of Lemma
D.2

We start by making the following simple observation: the tensor spectral norm is a
1
-Lipschitz function (w.r.t. the Frobenius norm) of the entries of the tensor. This follows since
\big|\|\bm{T}\|-\|\bm{R}\|\big|\leq\|\bm{T}-\bm{R}\|\leq\|\bm{T}-\bm{R}\|_{\mathrm{F}}
holds for any tensor
\bm{T},\bm{R}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d\times d}
. This allows us to invoke standard concentration results regarding functions of independent random variables.

We shall first develop an upper bound on the mean
\mathbb{E}\big[\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}(\bm{R})\big\|\big]
.
In view of
[
NDT15
, Corollary 4]
and Jensen’s inequality, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\left\|\bm{R}\right\|\big]\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big[\left\|\bm{R}\right\|^{2}\big]}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\Bigg(\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\max_{j,k\in[d]}\sum_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}+\max_{i,j\in[d]}\sum_{k\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}+\max_{i,k\in[d]}\sum_{j\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}\Bigg]\Bigg)^{1/2}\log^{5/2}d.

(257)

We then need to bound the quantity presented in (
257
).

For some
\beta>0
to be specified later, one can upper bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\max\nolimits_{j,k\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big\{\max_{j,k\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}>t\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\beta+\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big\{\max_{j,k\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}>t\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\beta+d^{2}\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}>t\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t.

(258)

We shall resort to the Bernstein inequality to bound
\mathbb{P}\big\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}>t\big\}
. It is straightforward to compute that

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i,j,k}^{2}\right]\leq\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}^{2},

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle:=\max_{i\in\left[d\right]}\left|R_{i,j,k}^{2}\right|\leq B^{2},

\displaystyle S^{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[R_{i,j,k}^{4}\right]\leq B^{2}\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}^{2}.

The Bernstein inequality then tells us that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}-M>t\right\}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{S^{2}},\frac{t}{L}\right\}\right),\quad t>0.

(259)

In particular, this implies that with probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-20}\right)
,

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M+L\log d+S\sqrt{\log d}\lesssim\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}^{2}+B^{2}\log d+\sqrt{B^{2}\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}^{2}\log d}

\displaystyle\asymp\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}^{2}+B^{2}\log d,

where we have used the AM-GM inequality in the last step.
Therefore, by taking

\displaystyle\beta:=C\left(\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}^{2}+B^{2}\log d\right)^{1/2}

for some sufficiently large constant
C>0
, we arrive at

\displaystyle\beta\geq\frac{C}{3}\left(M+L\log d+S\sqrt{\log d}\right)\gg M+L\log d+S\sqrt{\log d}.

(260)

Given that
\beta\gg M
, for any
t\geq\beta
one has the following relations about several events

\displaystyle\left\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}>t\right\}=\left\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}-M>t-M\right\}\subset\left\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}-M>t-\beta/2\right\}

(261)

In addition, it is easily seen that

\displaystyle\min\left\{t^{2}/S^{2},t/L\right\}\geq\frac{t}{\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}}

(262)

for any
t\geq\beta
(with
\beta
obeying (
260
)). As a result, one can bound

\displaystyle\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}>t\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(i)}}{\leq}\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}-M>t-\beta/2\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle=\int_{\beta/2}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\Big\{\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}-M>t\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(ii)}}{\leq}\int_{\beta/2}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{S^{2}},\frac{t}{L}\right\}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iii)}}{\leq}\int_{\beta/2}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\frac{t}{\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{16}\frac{\beta}{\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(iv)}}{\lesssim}\beta\exp\left(-\frac{1}{16}C\log d\right)\ll\beta/d^{2},

where (i) follows from (
261
), (ii) comes from (
259
), (iii) is a consequence of (
262
), and (iv) holds true when
C>0
is sufficiently large.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\max\nolimits_{j,k\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{i\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}\Big]\lesssim\beta\lesssim B\sqrt{\log d}+\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}.

Clearly, the same bound holds for
\mathbb{E}\Big[\max\nolimits_{i,k\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{j\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}\Big]
and
\mathbb{E}\Big[\max\nolimits_{i,j\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}R_{i,j,k}^{2}\Big]
.

Substitution into (
257
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\left\|\bm{R}\right\|\big]\lesssim B\log^{3}d+\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}\log^{5/2}d.

(263)

Recognizing that the magnitudes of all entries of
\bm{R}
are bounded by
B
