, we can invoke Talagrand’s concentration inequality
[
Ver18
, Theorem 5.2.16]
for convex Lipschitz functions of independent bounded random variables to show that with probability
1-O(d^{-10})
,

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\bm{R}\right\|-\mathbb{E}\big[\left\|\bm{R}\right\|\big]\right|\lesssim B\sqrt{\log d}

and, therefore,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{R}\right\|\lesssim B\log^{3}d+\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}\log^{5/2}d.

(264)

D.3.2
Proof of Corollary
D.3

Now we apply Lemma
D.2
to our concrete setting. We first look at
p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{T}^{\star}\right)-\bm{T}^{\star}
and treat it as
\bm{R}
in Lemma
D.2
. With the help of Lemma
D.1
, it is straightforward to compute that

\displaystyle\max_{i,j,k\in\left[d\right]}\left|T_{i,j,k}^{\star}\left(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1\right)\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\frac{\sqrt{\mu r}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}}{d^{3/2}p},

and

\displaystyle\max_{i,j\in\left[d\right]}\sum_{k\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\big[T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}\big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{d^{2}p}.

Clearly,
\max\nolimits_{i,k\in[d]}\sum\nolimits_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\big[T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}\big]
and
\max\nolimits_{j,k\in\left[d\right]}\sum\nolimits_{i\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\big[T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}\big]
can be controlled in the same way.
Substitution into (
264
) proves the claim (
246
).

We then turn to
\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\left(\bm{E}\right)
. Recognizing that the entries of
\bm{E}
might be unbounded, we invoke the following truncation trick to cope with this unboundedness issue. Specifically, define
\widetilde{\bm{E}}=[\widetilde{E}_{i,j,k}]_{1\leq i,j,k\leq d}
where

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}_{i,j,k}:={E}_{i,j,k}\ind\big\{\left|{E}_{i,j,k}\right|\leq c_{1}\sigma\sqrt{\log d}\big\},\qquad 1\leq i,j,k\leq d

(265)

for some some sufficiently large constant
c_{1}>0
. Moreover,
\widetilde{E}_{i,j,k}
is zero-mean because we assume that the distribution of
E_{i,j,k}
is symmetric about 0. Standard concentration inequalities reveal that: with probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
, one has
\bm{E}=\widetilde{\bm{E}}
. Hence, it suffices to bound
\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}(\widetilde{\bm{E}})\big\|
. Towards this end, simple calculation reveals that

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=\max_{i,j,k\in\left[d\right]}\big|\widetilde{E}_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\big|\lesssim\big\|\widetilde{\bm{E}}\big\|_{\infty}\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{\log d},

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mathsf{mode}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i,j\in\left[d\right]}\sum_{k\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\big[{E}^{2}_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\big]+\max_{i,k\in\left[d\right]}\sum_{j\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\big[{E}^{2}_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\big]+\max_{j,k\in\left[d\right]}\sum_{i\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\big[{E}^{2}_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\big]\lesssim p\sigma^{2}d.

This together with (
264
) as well as the high-probability event
\bm{E}=\widetilde{\bm{E}}
completes the proof.

D.4
Proof of Lemma
D.4

For notational simplicity, let us denote

\displaystyle\bm{X}

\displaystyle:=\left(p^{-1}\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\right)\times_{3}\bm{w}.

Observe that
\bm{X}
is a zero-mean random matrix in
\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}

with independent entries

\displaystyle X_{i,j}=\sum_{k\in\left[d\right]}w_{k}\left\{T_{i,j,k}^{\star}\left(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1\right)+p^{-1}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\right\},\quad\left(i,j\right)\in\left[d\right]^{2}.

We shall apply the truncated matrix Bernstein inequality to control
the spectral norm of
\bm{X}
.

•

First, it is straightforward to bound

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle:=\max\Big\{\max_{i\in\left[d\right]}\sum_{j\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i,j}^{2}\right],\max_{j\in\left[d\right]}\sum_{i\in\left[d\right]}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i,j}^{2}\right]\Big\}

\displaystyle\,=\max\Big\{\max_{i\in\left[d\right]}\sum_{j,k\in\left[d\right]}p^{-1}w_{k}^{2}\left(T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}+\mathbb{E}\left[E_{i,j,k}^{2}\right]\right),\max_{j\in\left[d\right]}\sum_{i,k\in\left[d\right]}p^{-1}w_{k}^{2}\left(T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}+\mathbb{E}\left[E_{i,j,k}^{2}\right]\right)\Big\}

\displaystyle\,\leq\frac{1}{p}\left(\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}d\right).

•

Second, using the same truncation argument as in the proof of Lemma
D.2
in Appendix
D.3
, we can assume
|E_{i,j,k}|\lesssim\sigma\sqrt{\log d}
for all
1\leq i,j,k\leq d
(which holds with very high probability). The Bernstein
inequality reveals that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X_{i,j}\right|>t\right\}\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{S^{2}},\frac{t}{L}\right\}\right),\quad t>0

for each
\left(i,j\right)\in\left[d\right]^{2}
, where

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle:=\max_{k\in\left[d\right]}\left\{\left|w_{k}\right|\left|T_{i,j,k}^{\star}\left(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1\right)+p^{-1}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\right|\right\}\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left(\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}+\sigma\sqrt{\log d}\right);

\displaystyle S^{2}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i,j}^{2}\right]\asymp\sum_{k\in\left[d\right]}p^{-1}w_{k}^{2}\left(T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}+\mathbb{E}\left[E_{i,j,k}^{2}\right]\right)\leq\frac{1}{p}\left(\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}\sigma^{2}\right).

This implies that with probability exceeding
1-O\left(d^{-20}\right)
,

\displaystyle\max_{i,j\in\left[d\right]}\left|X_{i,j}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim L\log d+S\sqrt{\log d}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\log d}{p}\left(\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}+\sigma\sqrt{\log d}\right)+\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{p}}\left(\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\sigma\right).

Therefore, if we choose

\displaystyle\beta:=C\left\{\frac{\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\log d}{p}\left(\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}+\sigma\sqrt{\log d}\right)+\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{p}}\left(\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\sigma\right)\right\}

for some sufficiently large constant
C>0
, then one has

\displaystyle\beta\geq\frac{C}{2}\left(L\log d+S\sqrt{\log d}\right).

•

Third, it is easy to bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\big[\left|X_{i,j}\right|\mathds{1}\left\{\left|X_{i,j}\right|\geq\beta\right\}\big]

\displaystyle\leq\beta\cdot\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X_{i,j}\right|\geq\beta\right\}+\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X_{i,j}\right|\geq t\right\}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\beta\cdot O\left(d^{-20}\right)+\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X_{i,j}\right|\geq t\right\}\mathrm{d}t.

In view of our choice of
\beta
, we know that
\min\left\{t^{2}/S^{2},t/L\right\}\geq t/\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}

for any
t\geq\beta
. As a result, for
d
sufficiently large, we
have

\displaystyle\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X_{i,j}\right|\geq t\right\}\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\min\left\{\frac{t^{2}}{S^{2}},\frac{t}{L}\right\}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\beta}^{\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\frac{t}{\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}\frac{\beta}{\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\max\left\{S/\sqrt{\log d},L\right\}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{8}C\log d\right)\ll\frac{\beta}{d^{2}}.

Consequently, we have established that

\displaystyle q:=\sum_{i,j}\mathbb{E}\big[\left|X_{i,j}\right|\mathds{1}\left\{\left|X_{i,j}\right|\geq\beta\right\}\big]\ll\beta.

Invoke the matrix Bernstein inequality to demonstrate that
with probability
1-O\left(d^{-10}\right)
,

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{X}\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim q+\beta\log d+\sqrt{V\log d}\asymp\beta\log d+\sqrt{V\log d}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\log^{2}d}{p}\left(\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}+\sigma\sqrt{\log d}\right)+\frac{\log^{3/2}d}{\sqrt{p}}\left(\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\sigma\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{p}}\left(\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\sigma\sqrt{d}\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\{\frac{\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\log^{2}d}{p}+\frac{\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}\log^{3/2}d}{\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{p}}+\frac{\sigma\log^{5/2}d}{p}\right\}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(\mathrm{i}\right)}{\lesssim}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\left\{\frac{\sqrt{\mu r}\log^{2}d}{d^{3/2}p}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r\log^{3}d}{d^{2}p}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{dp}}\right\}\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\frac{\sigma\log^{5/2}d}{p}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}}

\displaystyle\overset{\left(\mathrm{ii}\right)}{\lesssim}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r\log d}{dp}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\frac{\sigma\log^{5/2}d}{p}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{p}},

where (i) is due to Lemma
D.1
, and (ii) follows as long as
p\gtrsim d^{-2}\log^{3}d
and
\mu\log^{2}d\lesssim d
.

D.5
Proof of Lemma
D.5

Recall that for a standard Gaussian random variable
Z\sim\mathcal{N}\left(0,1\right)
, one has

\displaystyle\frac{1}{5t\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-t^{2}/2\right)\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \left(\frac{1}{t}-\frac{1}{t^{3}}\right)\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-t^{2}/2\right)\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq t\right\}\penalty\ \leq\penalty\ \frac{1}{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi}}\exp\left(-t^{2}/2\right)

(266)

for all
t>\sqrt{5/4}
.
Observing that
\kappa\sqrt{2\log r}+\Delta\geq\sqrt{5/4}
since
\kappa\geq 1
and
r\geq 2
, we can invoke the above tail bound to deduce that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,j}\geq\kappa\sqrt{2\log r}+\Delta\right\}

\displaystyle>\frac{1}{5\sqrt{2\pi}\left(\kappa\sqrt{2\log r}+\Delta\right)}\exp\left(-\big(\kappa\sqrt{2\log r}+\Delta\big)^{2}/2\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{5\sqrt{2\pi}\left(\kappa\sqrt{2\log r}+\Delta\right)r^{2\kappa^{2}}\exp(\Delta^{2})},

(267)

where we use the elementary inequality
\big(\kappa\sqrt{2\log r}+\Delta)^{2}\leq 4\kappa^{2}\log r+2\Delta^{2}
. In addition, it follows from the union bound that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\max_{1<i\leq r}\left|X_{i,j}\right|<\sqrt{2\log r}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-r\,\mathbb{P}\left\{\left|X_{i,j}\right|>\sqrt{2\log r}\right\}\geq 1-r\left\{\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\pi\log r}}\exp(-\log r)\right\}

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi\log r}}\geq 1-\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi\log 2}}.

(268)

To prove the claim, it is sufficient to choose
L
such that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big\{\forall j:X_{1,j}<\kappa\sqrt{2\log r}+\Delta\text{ or }\max\nolimits_{1<i\leq r}\big|X_{i,j}\big|\geq\sqrt{2\log r}\Big\}\leq\delta,

or equivalently,

\displaystyle\Big(1-\mathbb{P}\big\{X_{1,j}\geq\kappa\sqrt{2\log r}+\Delta\big\}\mathbb{P}\big\{\max\nolimits_{1<i\leq r}\left|X_{i,j}\right|<\sqrt{2\log r}\big\}\Big)^{L}\leq\delta.

(269)

Note that
\log(1-x)\leq-1/(2x)
for
0<x<1/4
. In view of (
267
) and (
268
), one can verify that the above inequality (
269
) as long as

\displaystyle L\geq C\big(\kappa\sqrt{\log r}+\Delta\big)r^{2\kappa^{2}}\exp(\Delta^{2})\,\log\frac{1}{\delta},

(270)

where
C>0
is some universal constant.

To prove the second claim, recall the definitions that

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}:=X_{1,j}-\max_{1<i\leq r}\kappa X_{i,j},\qquad 1\leq j\leq L.

and that
\Delta_{(1)}\geq\Delta_{(2)}\geq\dots,\geq\Delta_{(L)}
denote
\left\{\Delta_{j}\right\}_{j=1}^{L}
in descending order. For any
\epsilon>0
, one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\Delta_{(1)}-\Delta_{(2)}<\epsilon\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{1\leq j\leq L}\mathbb{P}\left\{\Delta_{j}-\max\nolimits_{k:k\neq j}\Delta_{k}<\epsilon\,\big|\,\Delta_{j}=\Delta_{(1)}\right\}\mathbb{P}\left\{\Delta_{j}=\Delta_{(1)}\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{\Delta_{1}-\max\nolimits_{k:k\neq 1}\Delta_{k}>\epsilon\,\big|\,\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{(1)}\right\},

where the last line holds because the distribution of
\Delta_{j}-\max\nolimits_{k:k\neq j}\Delta_{k}
conditional on
\Delta_{j}=\max\nolimits_{1\leq k\leq r}\Delta_{k}
is identical for all
1\leq j\leq L
. In addition, it is straightforward to see that

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{(1)}\quad

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad\Delta_{1}\geq\max_{k:k\neq 1}\Delta_{k}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\quad X_{1,1}\geq\max_{1<i\leq r}\kappa X_{i,1}+\max_{k:k\neq 1}\Delta_{k}=:\,Y_{1,1}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\Delta_{(1)}-\Delta_{(2)}<\epsilon\right\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{\Delta_{1}-\max\nolimits_{k:k\neq 1}\Delta_{k}<\epsilon\,\big|\,\Delta_{1}=\Delta_{(1)}\right\}=\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}-Y_{1,1}<\epsilon\,\big|\,X_{1,1}\geq Y_{1,1}\right\}.

Next, observe that
X_{1,1}
is independent of
Y_{1,1}
, and hence we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}-Y_{1,1}<\epsilon\,\big|\,X_{1,1}\geq Y_{1,1},Y_{1,1}=x\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}<x+\epsilon\,\big|\,X_{1,1}\geq y,Y_{1,1}=x\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}<x+\epsilon\,\big|\,X_{1,1}\geq x\right\}=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left\{x\leq X_{1,1}<x+\epsilon\right\}}{\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}\geq x\right\}}

for any
x\geq 0
. In order to study this function, we define

f_{\epsilon}(x):=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left\{x\leq Z\leq x+\epsilon\right\}}{\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x\right\}}
with
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
Taking the derivative of
f_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
w.r.t.
x
gives: for any
\epsilon>0
,

\displaystyle f^{\prime}_{\epsilon}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{\big\{\exp(-(x+\epsilon)^{2}/2)-\exp(-x^{2}/2)\big\}\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x\right\}+\exp\left(-x^{2}/2\right)\mathbb{P}\left\{x\leq Z\leq x+\epsilon\right\}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left(\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x\right\}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(-(x+\epsilon)^{2}/2\right)\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x\right\}-\exp(-\frac{x^{2}}{2})\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x+\epsilon\right\}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\left(\mathbb{P}\left\{N\geq x\right\}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(x^{2}/2\right)\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x\right\}-\exp\left((x+\epsilon)^{2}/2\right)\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x+\epsilon\right\}}{\sqrt{2\pi}\exp\left((x+\epsilon)^{2}/2\right)\exp\left(x^{2}/2\right)\left(\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x\right\}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}+2tx}{2}\right)-\exp\left(-\frac{t^{2}+2t(x+\epsilon)}{2}\right)\right)\,\mathrm{d}t}{2\pi\exp\left((x+\epsilon)^{2}/2\right)\exp\left(x^{2}/2\right)\left(\mathbb{P}\left\{Z\geq x\right\}\right)^{2}}>0.

In other words,
f_{\epsilon}(x)
is monotonically increasing in
x
for any given
\varepsilon>0
. Therefore, for any
0\leq x<B
for some sufficiently large
B>\sqrt{5/4}
(to be specified later), the above bounds taken together give

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}-Y_{1,1}<\epsilon\,\big|\,X_{1,1}\geq Y_{1,1},Y_{1,1}=x\right\}\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\leq}\frac{\mathbb{P}\left\{B\leq X_{1,1}<B+\epsilon\right\}}{\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}\geq B\right\}}\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\frac{\epsilon\exp\left(-B^{2}/2\right)}{\frac{1}{5B}\exp\left(-B^{2}/2\right)}=5\epsilon B,

where (i) arises from the monotonicity of
f_{\epsilon}(\cdot)
, and (ii) relies on (
266
). By taking
\epsilon=\delta/(5B)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}-Y_{1,1}<\epsilon\,\big|\,X_{1,1}\geq Y_{1,1},Y_{1,1}=x\right\}\leq\delta

for any
0\leq x\leq B
. Recall that
Y_{1,1}=\max_{1<i\leq r}\kappa X_{i,1}+\max_{k:k\neq 1}\Delta_{k}
. By standard Gaussian concentration inequalities, with probability at least
1-\delta
one has

\displaystyle Y_{1,1}\lesssim\kappa\sqrt{\log r}+\sqrt{\log L}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\asymp\sqrt{\log L}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}.

where the last step arises from the lower bound on
L
in (
270
). If we choose
B=C\big(\sqrt{\log L}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}\big)
for some sufficiently large universal constant
C>0
, then this immediately implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left\{\Delta_{(1)}-\Delta_{(2)}<\epsilon\right\}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}-Y_{1,1}<\epsilon\,\big|\,X_{1,1}\geq Y_{1,1}\right\}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\big\{Y_{1,1}>B\big\}+\mathbb{P}\left\{X_{1,1}-Y_{1,1}<\epsilon\,\big|\,X_{1,1}\geq Y_{1,1},Y_{1,1}=B\right\}\leq 2\delta.

We have therefore concluded the proof.

D.6
Proof of Lemma
D.6

To begin with, it is self-evident that

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}}(\bm{u}_{0})

\displaystyle=\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top}\bm{u}_{0}=\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{u}_{0}+\left(\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top}-\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\right)\bm{u}_{0}=\bm{u}_{0}+\left(\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top}-\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\right)\bm{u}_{0},

where the last identity follows since
\bm{u}_{0}
is assumed to lie within
\mathsf{span}(\bm{U})
.
As a result,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}^{\perp}}(\bm{u}_{0})

\displaystyle=\bm{u}_{0}-\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}}(\bm{u}_{0})=-\left(\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top}-\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\right)\bm{u}_{0}

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow\qquad\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}^{\perp}}(\bm{u}_{0})\big\|_{2}\leq\big\|\bm{V}\bm{V}^{\top}-\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\big\|\cdot\|\bm{u}_{0}\|_{2}\leq\delta.

The Pythagorean theorem then gives
\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}}(\bm{u}_{0})\big\|_{2}=\sqrt{\|\bm{u}_{0}\|_{2}-\big\|\mathcal{P}_{\bm{V}^{\perp}}(\bm{u}_{0})\big\|_{2}^{2}}\geq\sqrt{1-\delta^{2}}
.

D.7
Proof of Lemma
D.7

By virtue of Lemma
D.1
, we can compute

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\left[T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p}\sum_{i,j\in[d]}T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}\leq\frac{1}{p}\|\bm{A}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp}=:M;

\displaystyle\Big|T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p^{2}}\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\infty}^{2}=\frac{1}{p^{2}}\|\bm{A}^{\star}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{d^{3}p^{2}}=:L;

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}\Big(T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p^{3}}\sum_{i,j\in[d]}T_{i,j,k}^{\star 4}\leq\frac{1}{p^{3}}\|\bm{A}^{\star}\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\bm{A}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\lesssim\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4}}{d^{4}p^{3}}=:V.

Invoke the Bernstein inequality to show that: with probability exceeding

1-O(d^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M+L\log d+\sqrt{V\log d}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp}+\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log d}{d^{3}p^{2}}+\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4}\log d}{d^{4}p^{3}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp},

where the last line holds with the proviso that
p\gtrsim d^{-2}\log d
.

D.8
Proof of Lemma
D.8

Since the
E_{i,j,k}
’s are independent sub-Gaussian random variables with variance at most
\sigma^{2}
, one has

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\left[(E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}d^{2}p=:M;

\displaystyle\big\|(E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k})^{2}\big\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}=:L;

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}\left[(E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{4}d^{2}p=:V.

Here,
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
denotes the sub-exponential norm
[
Ver10
]
.
Taken together with the Bernstein inequality, these yield that with
probability exceeding
1-O(d^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[d]}(E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M+\sqrt{V\log d}+L\log^{2}d\lesssim\sigma^{2}d^{2}p+\sqrt{\sigma^{4}d^{2}p\log d}+\sigma^{2}\log^{2}d\asymp\sigma^{2}d^{2}p,

provided that
p\gtrsim d^{-2}\log^{2}d
.

D.9
Proof of Lemma
D.9

Fix an arbitrary
1\leq i\leq d
. We first define a sequence of independent zero-mean random variables
\{X_{j}\}_{j\in[d]}
as follows

\displaystyle X_{j}:=\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}T^{\star}_{i,j,k}w_{k}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1).

One can easily show that

\displaystyle\max_{k\in[d]}\Big|T^{\star}_{i,j,k}w_{k}(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)\Big|\leq\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}=:L,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{2}]=\sum_{k\in[d]}T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}w_{k}^{2}\mathbb{E}\big[(p^{-1}\chi_{i,j,k}-1)^{2}\big]\leq\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{k\in[d]}T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}=\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\|_{2,\infty}^{2}=:V.

The Bernstein inequality indicates that: with probability at least
1-O(d^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|X_{j}|\lesssim L\log d+\sqrt{V\log d}\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\log d+\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{p}}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}:=L_{j}.\end{split}

(271)

Moreover, we can also bound the variance of
X_{j}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathsf{Var}(X_{j}^{2})\leq\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{4}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p^{3}}\sum_{k\in[d]}T_{i,j,k}^{\star 4}w_{k}^{4}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\sum_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}T_{i,j,k_{1}}^{\star 2}T_{i,j,k_{2}}^{\star 2}w_{k_{1}}^{2}w_{k_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p^{3}}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{4}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\sum_{k\in[d]}T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{4}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\sum_{k\in[d]}T_{i,j,k}^{\star 2}.

Given that
X_{j}
might be overly large in some rare case, we introduce a sequence
\{Y_{j}\}
, where
we denote by
Y_{j}
the truncated version of
X_{j}
as follows

\displaystyle Y_{j}:=X_{j}\ind\{|X_{j}|\lesssim L_{j}\}.

We have learn from (
271
) and the union bound that with probability at least
1-O(d^{-15})
, one has
Y_{j}=X_{j}
for all
j\in[d]
.

Using the above bounds on the
X_{j}
’s, one observes that
\{Y_{j}\}_{j\in[d]}
is a sequence of independent random variables satisfying

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{2}]\leq\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2};

\displaystyle\max_{j\in[d]}Y_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j\in[d]}L_{j}^{2}\lesssim\frac{1}{p^{2}}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\log d+\frac{\log d}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2};

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}(Y_{j}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{4}]\lesssim\frac{1}{p^{3}}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{4}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{4}.

We can apply the Bernstein inequality to conclude that: with probability greater than
1-O(d^{-15})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}Y_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{2}]+\max_{j\in[d]}Y_{j}^{2}\log d+\sqrt{\sum\nolimits_{j\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}(Y_{j}^{2})\log d}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{\log^{2}d}{p^{2}}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{\log^{2}d}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{p^{3}}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{4}+\frac{\log d}{p^{2}}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{4}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{\asymp}\frac{1}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{\log^{2}d}{p^{2}}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}+\frac{\log^{2}d}{p}\left\|\bm{A}^{\star\top}\right\|_{2,\infty}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\lesssim}\left(\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp}+\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log^{2}d}{d^{3}p^{2}}+\frac{\mu^{2}r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}\log^{2}d}{d^{2}p}\right)\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{\mu r\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2}}{dp}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2},

where (i) is due to the AM-GM inequality,
(ii) makes use of Lemma
D.1
,
and the last line follows under the conditions
p\gtrsim d^{-2}\log^{2}d
and
\mu\log^{2}d\lesssim d
.
This together with the high-probability fact
Y_{j}=X_{j}
(
\forall j\in[d]
) concludes the proof.

D.10
Proof of Lemma
D.10

Fix any
1\leq i\leq d
. To begin with, define

Z_{j}:=\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}w_{k}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k},

which is a zero-mean random variable. In order to bound
Z_{j}
,
one observes that

\displaystyle\|w_{k}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\|_{\psi_{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}=:L;

\displaystyle\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}\mathsf{Var}\big(w_{k}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{2}p\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}w_{k}^{2}=\sigma^{2}p\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}=:V,

where
\|\cdot\|_{\psi_{1}}
denotes the sub-exponential norm.
Apply the Bernstein inequality for the sum of sub-exponential random
variables to obtain

|Z_{j}|\lesssim\sqrt{V\log d}+L\log^{2}d\lesssim\sigma\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}\sqrt{p\log d}+\sigma\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}\log^{2}d=:L_{j}

(272)

with probability exceeding
1-O(d^{-20})
. Further, given that
Z_{j}

is not necessarily bounded, we introduce a sequence of truncated random
variables as follows

Y_{j}:=Z_{j}\ind\{|Z_{j}|\lesssim L_{j}\}.

(273)

According to the above bound, one has
Y_{j}=Z_{j}
(
\forall j
)
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-19})
.

We then turn attention to bounding
\sum_{j\in[d]}Y_{j}^{2}
. To
this end, observe that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}[Y_{j}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}w_{k}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\Big)^{2}\Big]\lesssim\sigma^{2}p\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\in[d]}w_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sigma^{2}pd\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}=:M_{0}.

Additionally,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{j}^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\sum\nolimits_{k\in[d]}w_{k}E_{i,j,k}\chi_{i,j,k}\Big)^{4}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\in[d]}\mathbb{E}\Big[w_{k}^{4}E_{i,j,k}^{4}\chi_{i,j,k}^{4}\Big]+\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k_{1}\neq k_{2}}\mathbb{E}\Big[w_{k_{1}}^{2}w_{k_{2}}^{2}E_{i,j,k_{1}}^{2}E_{i,j,k_{2}}^{2}\chi_{i,j,k_{1}}^{2}\chi_{i,j,k_{2}}^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{4}p\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{k\in[d]}w_{k}^{4}+\sigma^{4}p^{2}\sum_{j\in[d]}\sum_{1\leq k_{1}\neq k_{2}\leq d}w_{k_{1}}^{2}w_{k_{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{4}pd\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}+\sigma^{4}p^{2}d\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{4}=:V_{0}.

Invokde the Bernstein inequality to arrive at: with probability at
least
1-O(d^{-20})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[d]}Y_{j}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim M_{0}+\sqrt{V_{0}\log d}+\max_{j\in[d]}L_{j}^{2}\log d

\displaystyle\lesssim\sigma^{2}pd\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sqrt{\sigma^{4}pd\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left(p\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\right)\log d}+\left(\sigma^{2}p\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}\log^{2}d+\sigma^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\log^{5}d\right)

\displaystyle\asymp\sigma^{2}pd\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\left\|\bm{w}\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\log^{5}d.

This together with the high-probability fact
Y_{j}=Z_{j}
(
\forall j
)
completes the proof.

Appendix E
Extension to asymmetric tensors

Thus far, we have focused on the case where the tensor of interest is symmetric.
In this section, we discuss how to generalize our algorithm
and analysis to accommodate asymmetric tensors.

E.1
Problem settings

Suppose that the unknown tensor
\bm{T}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times d_{2}\times d_{3}}

is a rank-
r
tensor with CP decomposition

\bm{T}^{\star}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star},

(274)

where
\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
,
\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}}
,

\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}}
represent the tensor factors
of interest. Apparently, there is an unavoidable global scaling ambiguity
issue (for instance, multiplying
\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}
by a constant
c
and multiplying

\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}

by
1/c
accordingly result in the same tensor). Without
loss of generality, we shall assume throughout that

\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}=\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}=\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2},\qquad 1\leq i\leq r.

(275)

In addition, we assume that each entry
(j,k,l)
is included in the sampling set
\Omega
independently with probability
p
, and that each observed entry
T_{j,k,l}^{\star}
is corrupted by an independent zero-mean sub-Guassian noise
E_{j,k,l}
(cf. Assumption
2.3
). Our goal is to (1) estimate
\{\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star},\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\}_{i=1}^{r}

faithfully, modulo global permutation and global signs, and (2) estimate
\bm{T}^{\star}
in a reliable manner.

E.2
Algorithms

We now move on to present an extension of our nonconvex algorithm to handle the noisy scenario.

First of all, setting

\displaystyle\bm{U}:=[\bm{u}_{1},\cdots,\bm{u}_{r}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r},\quad\bm{V}=[\bm{v}_{1},\cdots,\bm{v}_{r}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times r}\quad\text{and}\quad\bm{W}=[\bm{w}_{1},\cdots,\bm{w}_{r}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}\times r},

we can define the following regularized squared loss function

\displaystyle g(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{6p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\mathsf{reg}\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big),

(276)

where the regularization term
\mathsf{reg}\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big)

is given by

\mathsf{reg}\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big):=\frac{1}{24}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\left\{\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)^{2}+\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)^{2}+\big(\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)^{2}\right\}

(277)

for some positive regularization parameters
\{\alpha_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r}
to be specified
momentarily. In contrast to the symmetric case, the addition term
\mathsf{reg}\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big)

is included to help ensure that the sizes of
\bm{U},\bm{V}
and
\bm{W}

stay close — an algorithmic trick that has proved useful in other problems like nonconvex
rectangular matrix recovery
[
TBS+16
,
ZL16
,
CLL19
]
.

We are now ready to present our nonconvex algorithm
that accommodates the case with asymmetric tensors. As before, the
proposed algorithm is initialized by a spectral method, followed by
gradient descent designed to minimize the regularized loss function
(
276
). The precise procedure is described
in Algorithm
7
(which invokes Algorithms
8
-
9
).

Before proceeding, we find it helpful to record closed-form expressions for the gradients, which are a crucial part when implementing the nonconvex gradient descent algorithm.
Specifically, the gradients of
g(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})

can be computed as follows

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}_{i}}g\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3p}\,\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}\Big)\times_{2}\bm{v}_{i}\times_{3}\bm{w}_{i}+\frac{1}{6}\alpha_{i}\big(2\,\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\,\bm{u}_{i},

(278a)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{v}_{i}}g\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3p}\,\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}\Big)\times_{1}\bm{u}_{i}\times_{3}\bm{w}_{i}+\frac{1}{6}\alpha_{i}\big(2\,\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\,\bm{v}_{i},

(278b)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{w}_{i}}g\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3p}\,\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}\Big)\times_{1}\bm{u}_{i}\times_{2}\bm{v}_{i}+\frac{1}{6}\alpha_{i}\big(2\,\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\,\bm{w}_{i}

(278c)

for each
1\leq i\leq r
, where
\times_{1},\times_{2}

and
\times_{3}
have been defined in Section
2.4
.

Algorithm 7

Gradient descent for nonconvex tensor completion (asymmetric case)

1:

Generate initial
estimates
\bm{U}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r}
,
\bm{V}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times r}
,

\bm{W}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}\times r}
via Algorithm
8
.

2:

for

t=0,1,\dots,t_{0}-1

do

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}^{t}-\eta_{t}\nabla_{\bm{U}}g\big(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t},\bm{W}^{t}\big),

\displaystyle\bm{V}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\bm{V}^{t}-\eta_{t}\nabla_{\bm{V}}g\big(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t},\bm{W}^{t}\big),

\displaystyle\bm{W}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\bm{W}^{t}-\eta_{t}\nabla_{\bm{W}}g\big(\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t},\bm{W}^{t}\big),

where the gradients are given in (
278
).

Algorithm 8

Spectral initialization for nonconvex tensor completion (asymmetric
case)

1:

Let
\bm{U}\bm{\Lambda}\bm{U}^{\top}

be the rank-
r
eigen-decomposition of
\mathcal{P}_{\mathsf{off}\text{-}\mathsf{diag}}(\bm{A}\bm{A}^{\top})

where
\bm{A}=\mathsf{unfold}(\bm{T})
is the mode-1 matricization
of
\bm{T}
, and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathsf{off}\text{-}\mathsf{diag}}(\bm{Z})

extracts out the off-diagonal entries of
\bm{Z}
.

2:

Output:
initial estimates
\bm{U}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r}
,

\bm{V}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times r}
,
\bm{W}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}\times r}

on the basis of
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r}
using Algorithm
9
.

Algorithm 9

Retrieval of low-rank tensor factors from a given subspace estimate
(asymmetric case)

1:

Input:
number of restarts
L
, pruning threshold

\epsilon_{\mathsf{th}}
, subspace estimate
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r}

given by Algorithm
8
.

2:

for

\tau=1,\dots,L

do

3:

Generate an independent Gaussian vector
\bm{g}^{\tau}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\bm{I}_{d_{1}})
.

4:

\displaystyle\big(\bm{\nu}^{(1),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(2),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(3),\tau},\lambda_{\tau},\mathsf{gap}_{\tau}\big)\leftarrow\textsc{Retrieve-one-tensor-factor-asym}(\bm{T},p,\bm{U},\bm{g}^{\tau}).

5:

Generate tensor factor estimates

\displaystyle\big\{(\bm{u}^{1},\bm{v}^{1},\bm{w}^{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(\bm{u}^{r},\bm{v}^{r},\bm{w}^{r},\lambda_{r})\big\}\leftarrow\textsc{Prune-asym}(\big\{\big(\bm{\nu}^{(1),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(2),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(3),\tau},\lambda_{\tau},\mathsf{gap}_{\tau}\big)\big\}_{\tau=1}^{L},\epsilon_{\mathsf{th}}).

6:

Output:
initial estimate

\displaystyle\bm{U}^{0}=\big[\lambda_{1}^{1/3}\bm{u}^{1},\dots,\lambda_{r}^{1/3}\bm{u}^{r}\big],\qquad\bm{V}^{0}=\big[\lambda_{1}^{1/3}\bm{v}^{1},\dots,\lambda_{r}^{1/3}\bm{v}^{r}\big],\qquad\text{and}\qquad\bm{W}^{0}=\big[\lambda_{1}^{1/3}\bm{w}^{1},\dots,\lambda_{r}^{1/3}\bm{w}^{r}\big].

1:

function

Retrieve-one-tensor-factor-asym
(
\bm{T},p,\bm{U},\bm{g}
)

2:

Compute

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}

\displaystyle=\bm{U}\bm{U}^{\top}\bm{g}=:\mathcal{P}_{\bm{U}}(\bm{g}),

(279a)

\displaystyle\bm{M}

\displaystyle=p^{-1}\bm{T}\times_{1}\bm{\theta},

(279b)

where
\times_{1}
is defined in Section
2.4
.

3:

Let
\bm{\nu}^{(2)}
(resp.
\bm{\nu}^{(3)}
) be the leading
left (resp. right) singular vector of
\bm{M}
. Let
\bm{\nu}^{(1)}

=
p^{-1}\bm{T}\times_{2}\bm{\nu}^{(2)}\times_{3}\bm{\nu}^{(3)}

and set
\lambda=\|\bm{\nu}^{(1)}\|_{2}^{1/3}
.

4:

return

\big(\bm{\nu}^{(1)},\bm{\nu}^{(2)},\bm{\nu}^{(3)},\lambda,\sigma_{1}(\bm{M})-\sigma_{2}(\bm{M})\big)
.

1:

function

Prune-asym
(
\big\{\big(\bm{\nu}^{(1),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(2),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(3),\tau},\lambda_{\tau},\mathsf{gap}_{\tau}\big)\big\}_{\tau=1}^{L},\epsilon_{\mathsf{th}}
)

2:

Set
\Theta=\big\{\big(\bm{\nu}^{(1),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(2),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(3),\tau},\lambda_{\tau},\mathsf{gap}_{\tau}\big)\big\}_{\tau=1}^{L}.

3:

for

i=1,\dots,r

do

4:

Choose
(\bm{\nu}^{(1),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(2),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(3),\tau},\lambda_{\tau},\mathsf{gap}_{\tau})

from
\Theta
with the largest
\mathsf{gap}_{\tau}
; set
\bm{u}^{i}=\bm{\nu}^{(1),\tau},\bm{v}^{i}=\bm{\nu}^{(2),\tau},\bm{w}^{i}=\bm{\nu}^{(3),\tau}

and
\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{\tau}
.

5:

Update
\Theta\leftarrow\Theta\setminus\left\{\big(\bm{\nu}^{(1),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(2),\tau},\bm{\nu}^{(3),\tau},\lambda_{\tau},\mathsf{gap}_{\tau}\big)\in\Theta:|\langle\bm{\nu}^{(2),\tau},\bm{v}^{i}\rangle|>1-\epsilon_{\mathsf{th}}\right\}
.

6:

return

\big\{(\bm{u}^{1},\bm{v}^{1},\bm{w}^{1},\lambda_{1}),\dots,(\bm{u}^{r},\bm{v}^{r},\bm{w}^{r},\lambda_{r})\big\}.

E.3
Numerical experiments

In order to validate the effectiveness of the proposed algorithm,
we conduct a series of numerical experiments.

To begin with, let us generate the
true tensor
\bm{T}^{\star}=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}

via the following procedure: (1) generate
\widehat{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\overset{\mathsf{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{d_{1}})
,

\widehat{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\star}\overset{\mathsf{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{d_{2}})

and
\widehat{\bm{w}}_{i}^{\star}\overset{\mathsf{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(\bm{0},\bm{I}_{d_{3}})
,
and (2) set
\lambda_{i}^{\star}:=\|\widehat{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\widehat{\bm{w}}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}
,

\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}=\lambda_{i}^{\star 1/3}\widehat{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}
,

\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}=\lambda_{i}^{\star 1/3}\widehat{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\star}

and
\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}=\lambda_{i}^{\star 1/3}\widehat{\bm{w}}_{i}^{\star}
.
Akin to the symmetric case, we choose the algorithmic parameters to
be
L=r^{2}
,
\epsilon_{\mathsf{th}}=0.4
and
t_{0}=100
. The
noise components are generated as i.i.d. Gaussians, namely,
E_{i,j,k}\overset{\mathsf{i.i.d.}}{\sim}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
. The signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) is defined as
\text{SNR}=\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}/(\sigma^{2}d_{1}d_{2}d_{3})
,
and
\text{SNR}=\infty
stands for the noiseless case (i.e. the case with
\sigma=0
).
The stepsize is set to be
\eta_{t}\equiv\eta=1/\big(2\max_{i}\|\bm{u}_{i}^{0}\|_{2}^{4/3}\big)
,
where
\bm{U}^{0}=[\bm{u}_{1}^{0},\cdots,\bm{u}_{r}^{0}]
is the
initial estimate generated by Algorithm
8
.
Figure
2
illustrates the numerical convergence
rates of the proposed algorithm, where we set
d_{1}=100
,
d_{2}=150
,
d_{3}=200
,

r=4
and
p=0.05
. Since one can only recover the tensor factors
up to global signs and global permutation, the relative estimation
errors of
\bm{U}^{t}

are defined to be

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{\Pi}\in\mathsf{perm}_{r},\,\bm{S}=\mathsf{diag}(s_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},s_{i}=\pm 1}\left\|\bm{U}^{t}\bm{\Pi}\bm{S}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}/\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

\displaystyle\min_{\bm{\Pi}\in\mathsf{perm}_{r},\,\bm{S}=\mathsf{diag}(s_{i})\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r},s_{i}=\pm 1}\left\|\bm{U}^{t}\bm{\Pi}\bm{S}-\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{2,\infty}}/\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{2,\infty}},

where
\mathsf{perm}_{r}
stands for the set of
r\times r
permutation
matrices. The error metrics for
\bm{V}^{t}
and
\bm{W}^{t}
can be defined analogously. The relative Euclidean and
\ell_{2,\infty}
estimation
errors of
\bm{T}
are defined as
\|\bm{T}^{t}-\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}/\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

and
\|\bm{T}^{t}-\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}/\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{2,\infty}
,
respectively, where
\bm{T}^{t}=\sum_{1\leq i\leq r}\bm{u}_{i}^{t}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{t}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{t}
.
As can be seen from the plots, the estimation errors decay geometrically
fast in the noiseless case. In the noisy case, the numerical estimation
errors of the algorithm also converge geometrically fast until an
error floor is hit.

(a)

(b)

Figure 2
:
(a) relative errors of the estimates
\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t},\bm{W}^{t}

and
\bm{T}^{t}
vs. iteration count
t
for noiseless tensor completion,
where
d_{1}=100
,
d_{2}=150
,
d_{3}=200
,
r=4
,
p=0.05
;
(b) relative errors of the estimates
\bm{U}^{t},\bm{V}^{t},\bm{W}^{t}

and
\bm{T}^{t}
vs. iteration count
t
for noisy tensor completion,
where
d_{1}=100
,
d_{2}=150
,
d_{3}=200
,
r=4
,
p=0.05
,

\text{SNR}=10
.

Moving beyond the above synthetic data, we apply our methods to an simulated MRI brain
image dataset
[
CKK+97
]
, which is available online at the McGill University McConnell Brain Imaging
Centre and has also been studied in prior work
[
XYZ17
]
. The database consists of pre-computed simulated brain data, and a set of parameters can be set to generate the data accordingly. In this series of experiments, we choose the parameters to be T1 modality,
1
mm
slice thickness,
1\%
noise and
20\%
RF. The resulting
data is a three-order tensor in
\mathbb{R}^{181\times 217\times 181}
,
where each slice in any mode corresponds to a brain image. We use the tensor decomposition algorithm in
[
AGJ15
]
to decompose the original data, and keep the top-
36
components whose energy accounts for
85\%
of the original tensor. Hence, the resulting low-rank tensor
\bm{T}^{\star}
preserves the major information of the original tensor, and is used as the ground truth in the simulation. We sample
each entry of
\bm{T}^{\star}
independently with probability
p
,
and then inject i.i.d. Gaussian noise
\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
to each
observed entry (so as to simulate the noisy scenario). Based on the incomplete set of noisy samples, we apply the proposed algorithm to reconstruct
\bm{T}^{\star}
; the algorithmic parameters are chosen as above, and we denote by
\bm{T}
the resulting tensor estimate. Table
2

reports the relative errors
\|\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}/\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

and
\|\bm{T}-\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\infty}/\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\infty}
. As can be seen in the table, the performance of our algorithm is quite
favorable for low-rank tensor reconstruction. In particular, given that these real data tensor examples might not satisfy the assumptions imposed in our main theorems, these numerical experiments hint at the applicability of our algorithm to a broader set of problems.

and SNR in the MRI data experiments.

E.4
Analysis ideas

Before describing the proof ideas, we define the following incoherence
parameters and condition number that, similar to the symmetric case, play a crucial role in our theoretical
development.

Definition E.1
.

Define
the incoherence parameters and the condition number of
\bm{T}^{\star}
as follows

\displaystyle\mu_{0}

\displaystyle:=\frac{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\|\bm{T}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}},

(280a)

\displaystyle\mu_{1}

\displaystyle:=\max\left\{\frac{d_{1}\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}},\,\frac{d_{2}\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}},\,\frac{d_{3}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}}\right\},

(280b)

\displaystyle\mu_{2}

\displaystyle:=\max\left\{\frac{d_{1}\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{u}_{j}^{\star}\rangle^{2}}{\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{u}_{j}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}},\,\frac{d_{2}\langle\bm{v}_{i}^{\star},\bm{v}_{j}^{\star}\rangle^{2}}{\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{v}_{j}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}},\,\frac{d_{3}\langle\bm{w}_{i}^{\star},\bm{w}_{j}^{\star}\rangle^{2}}{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{w}_{j}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}}\right\},

(280c)

\displaystyle\kappa

\displaystyle:=\frac{\max_{i}\left\{\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\right\}}{\min_{i}\left\{\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\right\}}.

(280d)

For notational convenience, we shall set

\mu:=\max\left\{\mu_{0},\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\right\},\quad d_{\mathrm{min}}:=\min\{d_{1},\,d_{2},\,d_{3}\}\quad\text{and}\quad d_{\mathrm{max}}:=\max\{d_{1},\,d_{2},\,d_{3}\}.

(280e)

As has been made clear in the symmetric case, at the heart of our analysis lie two crucial components: (1) the
geometric property of a noiseless version of the loss function,
and (2) reasonably well initial estimates for tensor factors (in the entrywise sense).
Rather than providing a complete analysis for the asymmetric case (which would be very long), we shall only point out the important steps needed to extend these two parts for the asymmetric case.

1. Local optimization landscape.

Similar to the symmetric counterpart, the key step of the local convergence analysis lies in establishing the favorable geometric property (i.e. local strong convexity and smoothness) of the following noiseless regularized loss function

g_{\mathsf{clean}}\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big)=\frac{1}{6p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\mathsf{reg}\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big),

(281)

where the regularization term
\mathsf{reg}\big(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W}\big)

is defined in (
277
).
In words, this is a simplified version of the original loss function (
276
) by dropping the influence of the noise.
Lemma
E.2

below demonstrates that
g_{\mathsf{clean}}
is locally strongly
convex and smooth in the neighborhood of the ground truth.

Lemma E.2

(
Local strong convexity and smoothness).

Suppose that

\displaystyle p\geq c_{0}\max\left\{\frac{\log^{3}d_{\max}}{\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}},\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}d_{\max}\log^{5}d_{\max}}{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}\right\},\qquad r\leq c_{1}\sqrt{\frac{d_{\min}}{\mu}}

(282)

and that the regularization parameter obeys
\big|\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big|\leq c_{2}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}

for some sufficiently large (resp. small) constant
c_{0}>0
(resp.
c_{1},c_{2}>0
).
Then with probability greater than
1-O\big(d_{\min}^{-10}\big)
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{\top}\nabla^{2}g_{\mathsf{clean}}(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right),

(283)

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{\top}\nabla^{2}g_{\mathsf{clean}}(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

(284)

holds simultaneously for all
\bm{X}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r},\bm{Y}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times r},\bm{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}\times r}

and all
\bm{U}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r},\bm{V}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times r},\bm{W}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}\times r}

obeying

\displaystyle\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{S}^{(1)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star}\bm{S}^{(1)}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{U}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty};

(285a)

\displaystyle\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{\star}\bm{S}^{(2)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\left\|\bm{V}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{\star}\bm{S}^{(2)}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{V}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty};

(285b)

\displaystyle\|\bm{W}-\bm{W}^{\star}\bm{S}^{(3)}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\leq\delta\left\|\bm{V}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\quad\text{and}\quad\left\|\bm{W}-\bm{W}^{\star}\bm{S}^{(3)}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq\delta\left\|\bm{W}^{\star}\right\|_{2,\infty}.

(285c)

Here,
\delta\leq c_{3}/(\mu^{3/2}r)
for some
sufficiently small constant
c_{3}>0
, and
\bm{S}^{(1)}
,
\bm{S}^{(2)}

and
\bm{S}^{(3)}
are some diagonal matrices in
\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}

such that for each
1\leq i\leq r
, two of
S_{i,i}^{(1)}
,
S_{i,i}^{(2)}

and
S_{i,i}^{(3)}
equal to
-1
with the remaining one equal to
1
.

Proof.

See Appendix
E.5
.
∎

In a nutshell, Lemma
E.2
confirms local strong convexity and smoothness of the noiseless regularized loss
g_{\mathsf{clean}}(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})
,
provided that (
282
) holds and that the matrices
\bm{U}
,

\bm{V}
and
\bm{W}
are sufficiently close to the ground truth in every single
row. This is similar to the property of the symmetric counterpart
f_{\mathsf{clean}}(\bm{U})
(cf. Lemma
5.1
in Appendix
5.1
), except that we need to deal with asymmetric tensor factors as well as additional regularization terms. In particular, when
d_{1}\asymp d_{2}\asymp d_{3}\asymp d
and
\mu,r\asymp 1
, Condition (
282
) reduces to
p\gg d^{-3/2}\log^{3}d
and
r\ll\sqrt{d}
, which resembles (
42
) in Lemma
5.1
derived for the symmetric case.

Having established the preceding local geometric properties of
g_{\mathsf{clean}}
, one can then argue similarly as in Lemmas
5.3

and
5.6
in Appendix
5.1
to
prove that gradient descent converges linearly, as long as it is provided with an initial estimate satisfying the condition (
285
). Here, we emphasize that the regularization term (
277
), which essentially balances the sizes of the three tensor factors, is crucial for the local strong convexity and smoothness of
g_{\mathsf{clean}}
to hold.

2. Guaranteeing a reasonably good initialization.

Another crucial ingredient lies in guaranteeing an initial estimate with sufficiently good accuracy. Recall that our initialization scheme consists of two stages: (1) subspace estimation, and (2) retrieval of individual tensor factors.

•

The subspace estimation part remains largely unchanged: we shall unfold the observed tensor along the
1
-st mode to estimate the subspace spanned by tensor factors
\{\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\}_{i=1}^{r}
, and the
\ell_{2,\infty}
subspace estimation accuracy can be established by invoking the main theorems of our companion paper
[
CLC+20
]
.

•

Regarding the retrieval of individual tensor factors, the key observation is that: the random vector
\bm{g}^{\tau}
we generate satisfies

\displaystyle\bm{T}^{\star}\times_{1}\mathcal{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star}}(\bm{g}^{\tau})=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star}\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\mathcal{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star}}(\bm{g}^{\tau})\rangle\overline{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\star}\overline{\bm{w}}_{i}^{\star\top}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star}\langle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star},\bm{g}^{\tau}\rangle\overline{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\star}\overline{\bm{w}}_{i}^{\star\top},

where
\mathcal{P}_{\bm{U}^{\star}}
is the projection onto the subspace spanned by
\{\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\}_{i=1}^{r}
, and

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}=\frac{1}{\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\qquad\overline{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\star}=\frac{1}{\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}}\bm{v}_{i}^{\star},\qquad and\qquad\overline{\bm{w}}_{i}^{\star}=\frac{1}{\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}}\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}.

(286)

Given a sufficiently accurate subspace estimate
\bm{U}
for
\bm{U}^{\star}
obtained in the subspace estimation stage,
for each
1\leq i\leq r
, there exists at least a point
1\leq\tau\leq L

such that the spectral gap of the population version of
\bm{T}\times_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\tau}=\bm{T}\times_{1}\mathcal{P}_{\bm{U}}(\bm{g}^{\tau})

(with respect to the sampling and noise) is large enough and that the perturbation is sufficiently small. As a result, the top left (resp. right) singular vector
\bm{\nu}^{(2)}
(resp.
\bm{\nu}^{(3)}
) of
\bm{T}\times_{1}\bm{\theta}^{\tau}

is close to the (normalized) tensor factor

\overline{\bm{v}}_{i}^{\star}
(resp.
\overline{\bm{w}}_{i}^{\star}
)
both in the
\ell_{2}
and
\ell_{\infty}
norm (similar to Lemmas
5.13
-
5.14
in Section
5.3
for the symmetric case). In turn, this further allows
us to reliably estimate
\overline{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\star}
and the magnitude

\lambda_{i}^{\star}
, in a way similar to what we have done in Lemma
5.16
in Section
5.3
. By repeating the procedures with random restarts and invoking a pruning procedure similar to the symmetric case, we can hope to recover all tensor factors with high probability.

Following the above strategies, one could adapt the proofs of Theorems
5.9
-
5.10

in Section
5.3
to show that: our initial
estimates
\{\bm{u}_{i},\bm{v}_{i},\bm{w}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r}
are all exceedingly
close to the ground truth in the entrywise sense (up to global permutation
and global signs). This in turn confirms that the algorithm will enter a locally strongly convex and smooth region as characterized in Lemma
E.2
, thus leading to our performance guarantees for the entire algorithm. Once again, while the analysis ideas for the asymmetric case bear much resemblance to the symmetric counterpart, a complete proof has to be fairly long due to more clumsy notation compared to the symmetric case; for the sake of brevity, we do not provide the full proof here.

E.5
Proof of Lemma
E.2

Fix arbitrary matrices
\bm{X}=[\bm{x}_{1},\cdots,\bm{x}_{r}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r}
,

\bm{Y}=[\bm{y}_{1},\cdots,\bm{y}_{r}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times r}

and
\bm{Z}=[\bm{z}_{1},\cdots,\bm{z}_{r}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}\times r}
.
Direct computation reveals that

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{\top}\nabla^{2}g_{\mathsf{clean}}(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{3p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{2}{3p}\,\Big\langle\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{z}_{i},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}\rangle\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{w}_{i}\rangle\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{w}_{i}\rangle\big)^{2}+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big).

For notational convenience, we shall define

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}^{\bm{U}}=\big[\bm{\Delta}_{1}^{\bm{U}},\cdots,\bm{\Delta}_{r}^{\bm{U}}\big]:=\bm{U}\bm{S}^{(1)}-\bm{U}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times r},

and define
\bm{\Delta}^{\bm{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times r}
and

\bm{\Delta}^{\bm{W}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}\times r}
in an analogous
manner. Without loss of generality, it is assumed that
\bm{S}^{(1)}=\bm{I}_{r}

and
\bm{S}^{(2)}=\bm{S}^{(3)}=-\bm{I}_{r}
.

With the above notation in place, one can decompose

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{\top}\nabla^{2}g_{\mathsf{clean}}(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{4}\beta_{i}

where
\beta_{i},1\leq i\leq 4
are given respectively by

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{3}\,\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes(-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star})\otimes(-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star})+\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes(-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star})+\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes(-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star})\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\,\quad+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\Big[\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}+\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}+\big(\langle\bm{y}_{i},-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}\Big],

(287a)

\displaystyle\beta_{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{3p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\,\qquad-\frac{1}{3}\,\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes(-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star})\otimes(-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star})+\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes(-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star})+\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes(-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star})\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2},

(287b)

\displaystyle\beta_{3}

\displaystyle:=\frac{2}{3p}\,\Big\langle\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{z}_{i},\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big\rangle,

(287c)

\displaystyle\beta_{4}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\big(\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big)\Big[\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}+\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}+\big(\langle\bm{y}_{i},-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\,\quad+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\Big[\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle\big)^{2}+\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{W}}\rangle\big)^{2}+\big(\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{W}}\rangle\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\,\quad+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\Big[\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle\big)+\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{W}}\rangle\big)

\displaystyle\,\quad\qquad+\big(\langle\bm{y}_{i},-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},-\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)\big(\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{W}}\rangle\big)\Big]

\displaystyle\,\quad+\frac{1}{6}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\Big[\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)+\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\,\quad\qquad+\big(\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{w}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\Big].

(287d)

In what follows, we shall demonstrate that
\beta_{1}
is the dominant
term, with the remaining terms being negligible compared to
\beta_{1}
.
Here, we note that the proof idea is almost identical to that of the
symmetric case (cf. Lemma
5.1
in Appendix
5.1
).
For the
sake of conciseness, we will focus only on the part where the symmetric
and the asymmetric cases differ, and omit the proof details when their
analyses are similar.

Bounding
\beta_{1}

Let us first expand

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}\,\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}-\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}-\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\Big[\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle+\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}+\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle+\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}+\big(\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle-\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{1}{3}\,\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\,\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\,\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}}_{=:\,\gamma_{1}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle^{2}+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle^{2}+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle^{2}}_{=:\,\gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{-\frac{2}{3}\,\Big\langle\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star},\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\Big\rangle+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle}_{=:\,\gamma_{3}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{-\frac{2}{3}\,\Big\langle\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star},\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big\rangle+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle}_{=:\,\gamma_{4}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{2}{3}\,\Big\langle\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star},\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big\rangle-\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\langle\bm{z}_{i},\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\rangle}_{=:\,\gamma_{5}},

leaving us with five terms to control.

1.

Let us begin with
\gamma_{1}
. Observe that we can express

\displaystyle\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}=\big\|\bm{X}\bm{H}^{\top}\big\|_{\mathrm{F}},\qquad\text{with}\quad\bm{H}=[\bm{v}_{1}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{1}^{\star},\cdots,\bm{v}_{r}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{r}^{\star}]\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times r}

Arguing similarly as in the proof of Lemma
D.1
,
one can derive

\displaystyle\big|\sigma_{\min}(\bm{H})-\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}\big|\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big|\sigma_{\max}(\bm{H})-\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}\big|\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3},

provided that
r\ll d_{\min}/\mu
. This leads to the following inequalities

\displaystyle\frac{19}{20}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sigma_{\min}(\bm{H})\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\sigma_{\max}(\bm{H})\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{11}{10}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Clearly, the same argument reveals that

\displaystyle\frac{19}{20}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{11}{10}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}},

\displaystyle\frac{19}{20}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\frac{11}{10}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Combining these bounds, we reach

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}\leq\frac{3}{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

2.

We now move on to
\gamma_{2}
. Recall our assumption that
\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}=\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}=\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}=\lambda_{i}^{\star 1/3}

for all
1\leq i\leq r
. It follows from the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality
that

\displaystyle 0\leq\gamma_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\|\bm{x}_{i}\|^{2}\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\|\bm{y}_{i}\|^{2}\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\|\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

3.

Turning to
\gamma_{3}
, one can straightforwardly bound

\displaystyle|\gamma_{3}|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{i})}{=}\frac{2}{3}\Big|\sum_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\langle\bm{x}_{i_{1}},\bm{u}_{i_{2}}^{\star}\rangle\langle\bm{v}_{i_{1}}^{\star},\bm{y}_{i_{2}}\rangle\langle\bm{w}_{i_{1}}^{\star},\bm{w}_{i_{2}}^{\star}\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{ii})}{\leq}\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\big|\langle\bm{w}_{i_{1}}^{\star},\bm{w}_{i_{2}}^{\star}\rangle\big|\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\Big)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iii})}{\leq}\max_{i_{1}\neq i_{2}}\big|\langle\bm{w}_{i_{1}}^{\star},\bm{w}_{i_{2}}^{\star}\rangle\big|\|\bm{U}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{iv})}{\leq}r\sqrt{\frac{\mu}{d}}\,\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}

\displaystyle\overset{(\mathrm{v})}{\ll}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

Here, we have used the fact that
\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}=\lambda_{i}^{\star 1/3}

in (i); the inequalities (ii) and (iii) arise from the Cauchy-Schwartz
inequality; (iv) follows from (
280c
); (v) holds
as long as
r\ll\sqrt{d_{\min}/\mu}
. In a similar manner, one can
easily verify that

\displaystyle|\gamma_{4}|\ll\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad|\gamma_{5}|\ll\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\|\bm{Z}\|_{\mathrm{F}}.

It then follows from the AM-GM inequality that

\displaystyle|\gamma_{3}|+|\gamma_{4}|+|\gamma_{5}|\ll\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

4.

Putting the above bounds together allows us to bound
\beta_{1}

as follows

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+|\gamma_{3}|+|\gamma_{4}|+|\gamma_{5}|\leq\frac{7}{2}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right);

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle\geq\gamma_{1}-|\gamma_{3}|-|\gamma_{4}|-|\gamma_{5}|\geq\frac{9}{10}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

Bounding
\beta_{2}

To control
\beta_{2}
, we note that
\beta_{2}
involves two quantities:
(1) the deviation of
\frac{1}{p}\,\Big\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}\Big)\Big\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}

from its expectation with respect to
\Omega
; (2) the distance between

\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}+\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}

and
\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{x}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{y}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}-\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\otimes\bm{z}_{i}
.
The first term can be shown to be exceedingly small under our sample
size condition with the help of
[
YZ16
, Lemma 5]
,
while the second term is also guaranteed to be sufficiently small
by the assumptions of the error of
\bm{U},\bm{V}
and
\bm{W}

in (
285
).
Therefore, one can apply an analogous argument for
\alpha_{1}
and

\alpha_{2}
in the proof of Lemma
5.1
in Appendix
A

to derive: with probability at least
1-O\big(d_{\min}^{-10}\big)

one has

\displaystyle\big|\beta_{2}\big|\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right),

provided the sampling rate exceeds
p\gg\frac{\mu^{2}r^{2}d_{\max}\log^{2}d_{\max}}{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}
.

Bounding
\beta_{3}

By the definition of the operator norm, one
can bound

\displaystyle|\beta_{3}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big)\Big\|\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{r}\Big(\left\|\bm{w}_{i}\right\|_{2}\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}+\left\|\bm{v}_{i}\right\|_{2}\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}\|\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}+\left\|\bm{u}_{i}\right\|_{2}\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}\|\bm{z}_{i}\|_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{2/3}\,\Big\|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bm{u}_{i}\otimes\bm{v}_{i}\otimes\bm{w}_{i}-\bm{T}^{\star}\Big\|\Big\|p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}\big(\bm{1}_{d_{1}}\otimes\bm{1}_{d_{2}}\otimes\bm{1}_{d_{3}}\big)\Big\|\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

One can easily adapt the proof of Lemma
D.2

to show that, with probability at with probability at least
1-O\big(d_{\min}^{-10}\big),

\displaystyle\big\|\big(p^{-1}\mathcal{P}_{\Omega}-\mathcal{I}\big)\big(\bm{1}_{d_{1}}\otimes\bm{1}_{d_{2}}\otimes\bm{1}_{d_{3}}\big)\big\|\lesssim\frac{\log^{3}d_{\max}}{p}+\sqrt{\frac{d_{\max}\log^{5}d_{\max}}{p}}\lesssim\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}=\big\|\bm{1}_{d_{1}}\otimes\bm{1}_{d_{2}}\otimes\bm{1}_{d_{3}}\big\|

holds as long as
p\gg\max\Big\{\frac{\log^{3}d_{\max}}{\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}},\,\frac{d_{\max}\log^{5}d_{\max}}{d_{1}d_{2}d_{3}}\Big\}
.
We can then adapt the proof for bounding
\alpha_{3}

in the proof of Lemma
5.1
in Appendix
A
to derive that with probability at least
1-O\big(d_{\min}^{-10}\big),

\displaystyle\big|\beta_{3}\big|\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

Bounding
\beta_{4}

It remains to control
\beta_{4}
. By symmetry, it suffices to consider
the following terms:

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\big(\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big)\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle+\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2},

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle\big)^{2},

\displaystyle\gamma_{3}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle+\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle\big),

\displaystyle\gamma_{4}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big).

In the following, we shall bound these four terms separately. By the
assumptions that
\bm{S}^{(1)}=\bm{I}_{r},\bm{S}^{(2)}=\bm{S}^{(3)}=-\bm{I}_{r}
,

\delta\ll 1
and
\kappa\asymp 1
, one has

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\bm{U}-\bm{U}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\,\|\bm{U}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\sqrt{r}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 1/3}\leq\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2};

(288a)

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|-\bm{V}-\bm{V}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\,\|\bm{V}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\sqrt{r}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 1/3}\leq\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2};

(288b)

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{W}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|-\bm{W}-\bm{W}^{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\,\|\bm{W}^{\star}\|_{\mathrm{F}}\leq\delta\sqrt{r}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 1/3}\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 1/3}\leq\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}.

(288c)

In addition, for each
1\leq i\leq r
, one has

\frac{9}{10}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}\leq\lambda_{i}-\big|\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big|\leq\alpha_{i}\leq\lambda_{i}+\big|\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big|\leq\frac{11}{10}\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}

(289)

by virtue of the condition that
\big|\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big|\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}
.

1.

Let us start with
\gamma_{1}
. By the condition that
\max_{i}\big|\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big|\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 2/3}
,
it is easily seen that

\displaystyle|\gamma_{1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{r}\big|\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big|\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle^{2}+\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle^{2}\big)\leq\max_{i}\big|\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}\big|\alpha_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{\star 2/3}\big|\left(\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\big(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\big(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big),

with the proviso that
\kappa\asymp 1
.

2.

As for
\gamma_{2}
, one can bound

\displaystyle|\gamma_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle^{2}-\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle^{2}\big)\leq\max_{i}\alpha_{i}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\big(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big).

Here, we use (
288a
), (
288b
),
(
289
) and
\kappa\asymp 1
in the last
step.

3.

Turning to
\gamma_{3}
, one can develop a similar bound as follows

\displaystyle|\gamma_{3}|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\rangle+\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\rangle\big)^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle-\langle\bm{y}_{i},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle\big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\max_{i}\alpha_{i}\sqrt{\left(\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\big(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)}\cdot\sqrt{\big(\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big)}

\displaystyle\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\big(\|\bm{X}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\|\bm{Y}\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\big).

4.

With regards to
\gamma_{4}
, we can expand

\displaystyle|\gamma_{4}|

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}+\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|-\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}+\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+2\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle+\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+2\langle\bm{v}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle-\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(2\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle+\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}+2\langle\bm{v}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle-\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big),

where the last step follows from the assumption
\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}=\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}=\|\bm{w}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{v}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\big(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{i=1}^{r}\alpha_{i}\big|\langle\bm{u}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\rangle+\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}^{2}+\langle\bm{v}_{i}^{\star},\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\rangle+\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\|_{2}^{2}\big|\left(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}\alpha_{i}\big(\|\bm{u}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{U}}\|_{2}+\|\bm{v}_{i}^{\star}\|_{2}\|\bm{\Delta}_{i}^{\bm{V}}\|_{2}\big)\sum_{i=1}^{r}\left(\|\bm{x}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bm{y}_{i}\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right),

where we have used the conditions that
\max_{i}\alpha_{i}\lesssim\lambda_{\max}^{\star 2/3}

and
\kappa\asymp 1
.

5.

It is not hard to check that similar bounds also hold for the remaining
terms in
\beta_{4}
. As a result, we arrive at

\displaystyle\big|\beta_{4}\big|\ll\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right).

Combining the previous bounds on
\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\beta_{3}

and
\beta_{4}

Putting the above estimates together, we conclude that with probability
at least
1-O(d_{\min}^{-10})
, one has

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{\top}\nabla^{2}g_{\mathsf{clean}}(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\beta_{1}-|\beta_{2}|-|\beta_{3}|-|\beta_{4}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\lambda_{\min}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)^{\top}\nabla^{2}g_{\mathsf{clean}}(\bm{U},\bm{V},\bm{W})\,\mathsf{vec}\left(\begin{bmatrix}\bm{X}\\
\bm{Y}\\
\bm{Z}\end{bmatrix}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\beta_{1}+|\beta_{2}|+|\beta_{3}|+|\beta_{4}|\leq 4\lambda_{\max}^{\star 4/3}\left(\left\|\bm{X}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Y}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}+\left\|\bm{Z}\right\|_{\mathrm{F}}^{2}\right)

as claimed.
